linear motion

RAIL_SLOT_INSET15 mmmeasured
used by RAIL_LOW_Z, RAIL_HIGH_Z, SIDE_TSLOT_ROW_1, STRIP_ROW_LOW, STRIP_ROW_HIGH, STRIP_HEAD_REACH
source machine/gantry-beam-joint.md, measured table, confirmed 2026-10-01
The two rails on a beam go in the OUTSIDE T-slot of each stacked extrusion - X, Y1 and Y2 all the same, confirmed by him again 2026-10-03. On a 30-6060 that slot centre is 15 from the extrusion's outer face, the same 15 as EXT_BORE_INSET and for the same reason: the 30 series puts both on the module grid. NOT NEW: gantry-beam-joint.md's measured table has carried 'rail spacing X 90 mm, the outermost of the four front slots, at 15 and 105 up the section' since 2026-10-01. It had simply never been lifted into the registry, which is how a question already closed on the bench came to be asked again.
RAIL_LOW_Z15 mmalias
another name for RAIL_SLOT_INSET
used by RAIL_PITCH, RAIL_LOW_XEP
Lower rail centreline, measured from the beam's BOTTOM face. Beam-local, so it reads the same on X, Y1 and Y2. OPEN: YES, IT IS A STRAIGHT COPY OF RAIL_SLOT_INSET and the two are both 15 - his question, 2026-10-11. The alias earns its place by naming a DIFFERENT THING that happens to share the value: RAIL_SLOT_INSET is how far a slot centre sits from the extrusion's outer face, and this is where the lower rail ends up measured from the beam's bottom. They coincide only because the bottom rail sits in the first slot up. SETTLED: ITS SIBLING IS WHY IT IS WORTH HAVING: RAIL_HIGH_Z is BEAM_H - RAIL_SLOT_INSET, and the pair then reads as two centrelines in one frame, so RAIL_PITCH is RAIL_HIGH_Z - RAIL_LOW_Z rather than a subtraction that mixes a beam height with a slot inset. FLAG: IT IS NOT FREE: if the bottom rail ever moves to another slot this has to stop being an alias, and the thing that would hide that is exactly how alike they look now.
RAIL_HIGH_Z105 mmderived
built from BEAM_H - RAIL_SLOT_INSET
used by RAIL_PITCH, RAIL_HIGH_XEP, X_BEAM_TOP_XC
Upper rail centreline, from the beam's bottom face. 105.
RAIL_PITCH90 mmderived
built from RAIL_HIGH_Z - RAIL_LOW_Z
used by BLOCK_ROW_GAP_GEOM
90, and it agrees exactly with the measured 90 in gantry-beam-joint.md. The outside slot is used because it is the widest the pair can be spread on a 120 beam; this is the couple that resists nod, so it is a stiffness number, not a convenience. It is also what lays out Sheet 2a's sixteen block holes - see BLOCK_ROW_GAP for what does not.

BLOCK_ROW_GAP45 mmmeasured
Facing edges of the upper and lower Y block rows, measured. OPEN: IT DOES NOT LAY OUT THE 16 BOLT HOLES - settled by him 2026-10-03. The rows are set by the rails, which sit in the beams' OUTSIDE T-slots and are therefore RAIL_PITCH apart; the geometric gap that follows is BLOCK_ROW_GAP_GEOM at 46. What it actually describes is a KEEP-OUT, his words 2026-10-03: the magnetic linear encoder tape runs down this gap and the 35 dia sensor rides over the tape, both centred on the beam's seam. Keep it at 45 - under-stating a keep-out is safe. THREE NUMBERS EXIST FOR THIS GAP and they do not agree: 45 here, 46 from the geometry (BLOCK_ROW_GAP_GEOM = 90 rail pitch less a 44 block), and 46.7 measured at a trial fit on the X beam's front face with a photo behind it, in gantry-beam-joint.md. On a 90 pitch those imply block widths of 45, 44 and 43.3. HGH20 is 44 nominal, so the geometric 46 is the one to believe and both measurements are loose. It does not matter: nothing is laid out from this, and the smallest value is the safe one for a keep-out. Using it to lay holes out from, which is what happened until 2026-10-03, put all sixteen holes 0.5 out.
BLOCK_FACE_CLEAR4.2 mmmeasured
4.2 - the gap under the HGH20 block's SIDE WINGS, from their bottom edge to the face of the extrusion the rail is bolted to. SETTLED: HIS MEASUREMENT 2026-10-11, taken with precision steel setup gauge blocks, and it replaces the 4 that was known only as 'a 4 mm test piece slides under'. FLAG: IT IS WHAT LETS THE FRONT STRIP RUN UNDER THE BLOCKS: T_FRONT_STRIP is 3 measured, so a strip passes with 1.2 to spare and FRONT_STRIP_W's 50.8 can be wider than the block gap on purpose. OPEN: THE MODEL DREW THE BLOCK AS A SOLID BOX filling all RAIL_STACK from the face until this figure arrived, which is why the interference check reported six front-strip clashes that are not real. A thicker strip would foul, and the check will say so.
BLOCK_W44 mmpart
used by BLOCK_ROW_GAP_GEOM, X_DOUBLER_BLOCK_CLEAR, DOUBLER_BLOCK_CLEAR
HGH20 block body width, measured. Only used for clearance - what is left between the rows, and where the lower row's bottom edge falls against the doubler.
BLOCK_ROW_GAP_GEOM46 mmderived
built from RAIL_PITCH - BLOCK_W
The gap the geometry actually produces once the rails are in the outside T-slots: 46. One more than the measured 45, which is why 45 is kept as the conservative clearance number rather than corrected.
BEAM_SEAM_XEP125 mmderived
built from XEP_SOLE_TAB + NUT_FACE_BELOW_BEAM + EXT_W

RAIL_LOW_XEP80 mmderived
built from XEP_SOLE_TAB + NUT_FACE_BELOW_BEAM + RAIL_LOW_Z

plate thicknesses

T_X_ENDPLATE12.19 mmmeasured
used by T_DOUBLER, SCREW_AXIS_X, NUT_BOLT_X_NEAR, NUT_BOLT_X_FAR, SOLE_REACH, NUT_AIR_CLEAR, SOLE_BRACKET_L, ROOT_BOLT_X_ENDPLATE, THRUST_ARM, DOUBLER_SEATING, Y_BEAM_GAP, DOUBLER_BOLT_ENGAGE
source machine/end-plates-risers-and-spindle.md
1/2in 6061, HIS MICROMETER in several places 2026-10-07: 12.19, not the 12.7 nominal this was carried at. That is 0.48in stock sold as half inch, and 0.61 per plate - so 1.22 across the machine, which is why MACHINE_OUTER_W moved - it was called REAR_PLATE_L then. The entry had said CALIPER IT because two other nominals had already been wrong; this is the third.
T_DOUBLER12.19 mmalias
another name for T_X_ENDPLATE
used by X_SOLE_ROOT_ROW, SOLE_REACH, NUT_AIR_CLEAR, ROOT_BOLT_X_DOUBLER, DOUBLER_SEATING, DOUBLER_BOLT_BREAKOUT, SOLE_BASE_D
The doubler IS the end plate's offcut - 200 wide stock less the plate's 154 leaves the 45 strip, which is where DOUBLER_H comes from. So it is the same piece of metal and cannot have its own thickness. It was carried as an independent 12.7 and would have stayed at 12.7 when the end plate went to 12.19, which is the drift this expression removes.
T_SOLE_BRACKET6.35 mmnominal
used by SOLE_ROOT_ENGAGE, Y_NUT_BOLT_ENGAGE
1/4in STEEL. NOMINAL AND IT HAS TO STAY NOMINAL: the shop supplies this plate, so there is nothing to micrometer the way T_REAR_PLATE's 1/4in came back 6.19. A 6.19 here would only lengthen BOLT_Y_NUT_ENGAGE, which is slack in the safe direction.
T_INTERPOSER10 mmpart
used by X_NUT_FACE_Z, BF12_ROW1_Y, TONGUE_BF12_ROW1, TONGUE_MIN, Y_SCREW_BELOW_BEAM, CASTING_RAIL_BOLT_L, CASTING_BLOCK_BOLT_L, CAST_HEAD_Z_UP
10 mm, HIS PART 2026-10-08 - metric plate, NOT the 3/8in (9.525) this was carried at as a nominal. FLAG: SECOND PART ON THIS MACHINE TO COME BACK METRIC WHERE THE FILE ASSUMED IMPERIAL, and from the same direction: T_FIN was corrected to a measured 10 for the fin square on 2026-10-02 with the same words - not 3/8in and not 1/2in. It feeds 21 symbols, so it is the single highest-reach nominal in this file and it is now his number rather than a fraction it was sold as. OPEN: CALIPER IT ON ARRIVAL anyway: 10 nominal is still a nominal, and the two parts it most affects - the BF12 row and the X end plate stack - are both one-shot.
T_FIN10 mmmeasured
used by BOX_FRONT_OVERHANG, FIN_BOLT_TAP_DEPTH, FIN_ANGLE_TO_RIB, FIN_TNUT_TO_RIB

His caliper on the fin stock: 10, metric plate, NOT 3/8in (9.525) and not 1/2in. OPEN: THIS NOTE SAID 'the 302 square' AND BOTH HALVES OF THAT WERE OVERTAKEN - the piece measures 301.625 (PLATE_H), and as of 2026-10-08 each fin has its own piece rather than both nesting out of one square. The 10 is the part of the reading that was ever about thickness and it is unchanged.
T_SIDE_PLATE3.39 mmmeasured
used by FIN_PROUD, Y_BEAM_ASSY_W, Y1_EXT_FACE_X, T_OUTBOARD_PLATE, TT_BED_CLEAR, TT_BED_SCREW_STACK, TT_BED_GROOVE_INSET
source Specialty Metals Corp sales order W053768, 2026-10-08
3.39 - MICROMETER, the delivered sheet. It supersedes the sales order, which read CSH 10GA(.135) and gave 3.429: 10 gauge is a gauge, the gauge is a nominal, and this one came in 0.039 under it. FLAG: IT FEEDS 14 SYMBOLS and the two that matter are MACHINE_OUTER_W, the flush envelope, which is two of these wide, and the tee-track bed chain, where TT_BED_FLOAT has the least room of anything it touches. OPEN: T_OUTBOARD_PLATE IS AN ALIAS OF THIS. If the outboard plates came off a different sheet they need their own reading rather than inheriting one taken on this one.
T_BACK_JOIN_PLATE6.35 mmpart
source Specialty Metals Corp sales order W053768, 2026-10-08
1/4in 6061, X back joining plate across the beam seam - ORDERED as a true 1/4in, same call as T_REAR_PLATE. The sales order line reads APL 0.25in x 4.6875in x 39.3125in 6061 T651, one piece, so the temper is T651 and the piece is sheared 119.0625 x 998.5375. OPEN: SHEET 10 DRAWS IT 120 x 1000 and the ordered piece is neither: see XBJ_W and XBJ_L.
XBJ_W119.062 mmpart
source Specialty Metals Corp sales order W053768, 2026-10-08
119.0625, the ORDERED width of the X back joining plate - 4 11/16in, sheared. OPEN: SHEET 10 DRAWS IT 120 AND CALLS THAT 'the full section height', which is the one claim the ordered piece breaks: it is 0.9375 shy of the 30-6060 pair's 120. It costs nothing because Sheet 10 is referenced to the plate's TOP edge, which is the beam's top face, so the four T-slot rows at 15 / 45 / 75 / 105 do not move and the shortfall lands at the BOTTOM, 14.06 below the last row instead of 15. FLAG: DO NOT RE-REFERENCE SHEET 10 TO ITS BOTTOM EDGE to 'fix' this - the top edge is the reference precisely because it is the face that registers against the beam, and the bottom edge of this plate registers against nothing.
XBJ_L998.538 mmpart
source Specialty Metals Corp sales order W053768, 2026-10-08
998.5375, the ORDERED length of the X back joining plate - 39 5/16in, sheared, the same shear length as the two side plates. Sheet 10 draws it 1000. Same shape of slack as SIDE_PLATE_L and the same reason it is harmless: the seven stations run 50 to 950, so 1.46 of margin disappears from the end the plate is not laid out from.

the Y ball nut chain

BK12_STANDOFF_ASBUILT5 mmmeasured
BK12/BF12 mounted 5 above the extrusion, AS BUILT. FLAG: DO NOT DELETE because the standoff no longer exists in the design - that is exactly why this is here. NUT_FACE_BELOW_BEAM_ASBUILT was measured WITH the standoff, so converting that measurement to the design has to take the 5 back off before adding the interposer. Removing this symbol would silently resize the X end plate: it feeds XEP_H, which is the 242.5.
NUT_FACE_BELOW_BEAM_ASBUILT58 mmmeasured
source measured 2026-10-02
Beam underside to the ball nut's downward mounting face, as built with the 5 mm standoff. FLAG: SUPERSEDED 2026-10-10 AND NOTHING DERIVES FROM IT NOW. It used to feed NUT_FACE_BELOW_BEAM, which now runs on the screw axis instead. Kept rather than deleted because it records a disagreement worth remembering: taken with the 5 standoff, a nut h of 20 puts the as-built screw axis 38 below the beam, where that machine's own supports - 5 of standoff plus a 25 bore - put it at 30. It was 8 out against its own geometry before anyone tried to convert it, which is what finally condemned it.
NUT_FACE_BELOW_BEAM55 mmderived
built from Y_SCREW_BELOW_BEAM + NUT_AXIS_TO_FACE
used by BEAM_SEAM_XEP, RAIL_LOW_XEP, RAIL_HIGH_XEP, XEP_H, XEP_BOTTOM
55 - the ball nut's downward bolting face below the Y beam. FLAG: IT WAS 63 UNTIL 2026-10-10 and it is now built the other way round. It used to start from NUT_FACE_BELOW_BEAM_ASBUILT, a 58 calipered on the machine as it was built with the 5 mm standoff, and convert that to the design. It now starts from where the screw actually runs: Y_SCREW_BELOW_BEAM, which is his 35, plus the nut's own h. The old route disagreed with the supports by 8 and the supports won - BF12 and the casting are both spec parts and the 58 was one reading of a machine that no longer exists in that form. OPEN: THIS MOVES XEP_H, so the X gantry end plate on Sheet 2a gets 8 shorter. It moves no hole against the beam - the rail and block figures are beam-local underneath and only their coordinates from the plate edge change. SETTLED: CONFIRMED INDEPENDENTLY by him on 2026-10-10: the nut face stands 55 off the beam, below on Y and above on X, both being the same 10 of interposer plus 25 to the bore plus 20 of nut. That is also footnote 3.8's X chain, which already added those three to put the X doubler's top edge at 222 - so the 55 was in the prose before it was in this symbol.
XEP_H235 mmderived
built from XEP_TOP_ABOVE_BEAM + BEAM_H + NUT_FACE_BELOW_BEAM
used by XEP_CUT_CHECK
235 - the MINIMUM height the X end plate has to reach, its bottom level with the ball nut's mounting face so the doubler and sole bracket can get to it. FLAG: IT IS A FLOOR AND NOT THE CUT, which is XEP_CUT. Those were the same symbol until 2026-10-10 and the conflation hid two drifts at once: T_INTERPOSER went 9.525 to 10 and took this from 242.525 to 243 without Sheet 2a hearing about it, and then NUT_FACE_BELOW_BEAM came down 8. Running a cut off a floor means every time the floor moves, a plate silently wants recutting. SETTLED: The 242.5 cut clears this by 7.5 and that is spare metal, nothing more - his, 2026-10-10: no hole moves because of XEP_H.
XEP_CUT_CHECK0 mmderived
built from XEP_CUT - (XEP_H + XEP_SOLE_TAB)
0 - and it must stay 0. The plate is cut to the nut plane plus the tab; nothing else is a legal length. NEGATIVE means the plate does not reach the nut face at all.
XEP_SOLE_TAB10 mmpinned
used by BEAM_SEAM_XEP, RAIL_LOW_XEP, RAIL_HIGH_XEP, XEP_CUT_CHECK, XEP_BOTTOM, DOUBLER_BOTTOM_X, CORNER_CUT_X_END
10 - how far the X end plate reaches PAST the doubler's end face, so the sole plate's base has a face to sit against. His. The Y nut joint is the X carriage's upside down: the doubler's end face is level with the nut's mounting face, the sole plate lies across that face and bolts into its edge, and the plate carries on past to back the base. FLAG: THE PLATE'S BOTTOM EDGE IS THEREFORE NOT THE NUT PLANE, and every coordinate measured up the plate carries this term. At 0 the sole plate has nothing to locate against. It does not change DOUBLER_BLOCK_CLEAR - the doubler and the block row both shift up with it.
XEP_BOTTOM-185 mmderived
built from 0 - (BEAM_H + NUT_FACE_BELOW_BEAM + XEP_SOLE_TAB)
Plate bottom, measured from the Y beam top. Negative is down. It reaches XEP_SOLE_TAB below the nut plane so the sole plate's base has a face to sit against.
SCREW_AXIS_X72.19 mmderived
built from T_X_ENDPLATE + RAIL_STACK + (EXT_W / 2)
used by NUT_BOLT_X_NEAR, NUT_BOLT_X_FAR, SOLE_REACH, NUT_AIR_CLEAR, SOLE_BRACKET_L, THRUST_ARM
Y screw centreline, from the X end plate's INNER face. The (EXT_W / 2) term is BY CONSTRUCTION, not an assumption: the BK12 and BF12 support blocks are 60 wide and the BF12 tongue is cut 60 wide to match, so the screw is centred in 60 by the parts themselves. Reconciled against a measurement 2026-10-03: it leaves 21.81 of air past the doubler, which meets the 20 minimum.
NUT_FACE_X52 mmvendor
used by X_SOLE_BRACKET_L, NUT_INSET_X, SOLE_REACH, NUT_AIR_CLEAR, SOLE_BRACKET_L
source Vevor DSG1605 page, printed manual with the ball screw kit
Ball nut housing across the long way - B on the DSG1605 page, 52. Parallel with the X beam. SETTLED: It had been carried since 2026-10-02 with a date and no source; the page turned up 2026-10-09 and confirms it.
NUT_FACE_Y40 mmvendor
used by NUT_INSET_Y, SOLE_TIP_W
source Vevor DSG1605 page, printed manual with the ball screw kit
Ball nut housing's mounting flat along the screw axis - L on the DSG1605 page, 40. With NUT_FACE_X's B of 52 this is the flat the sole plate lands on, 52 x 40. OPEN: IT IS L, NOT H, AND I FIRST WROTE H: the page carries BOTH at 40, so nothing numeric moves, but they are different dimensions and conflating them would mislead the next reader. H is the housing's width in the face view and it is the one NUT_AXIS_TO_FACE is half of; L is the length along the screw and it is the one C1 and C2 divide up. SETTLED: The check that settles which: NUT_INSET_Y comes out at 8, which is C1 - and C1 is an inset along L.
NUT_PATTERN_X40 mmvendor
used by X_NUT_BOLT_NEAR, X_NUT_BOLT_FAR, NUT_INSET_X, NUT_BOLT_X_NEAR, NUT_BOLT_X_FAR, Z_NUT_Y_1, Z_NUT_Y_2
40 - the pattern ACROSS the housing's mounting flat, P on the DSG1605 page, and the span a sole plate's clearance holes have to match. 4 x M5 THREADED INTO THE HOUSING, not through-holes. SETTLED: CLOSED 2026-10-09 AFTER A FALSE ALARM WORTH RECORDING: the page is also labelled 6-Y, six M5 on a øW 38 circle, and 38 cannot be the spacing of four holes pitched 40, so the two looked irreconcilable. They are different interfaces. THE SIX ARE NUT_SFU_PATTERN - the housing bolting to the SFU ball nut's own flange, internal to the screw assembly and nothing to do with the machine. THE FOUR ON THE FLAT ARE THE MACHINE INTERFACE, and they are the ones in the LEFT view. SETTLED: The four-hole reading is confirmed by arithmetic the page closes on itself: C1 + C2 + C1 = 8 + 24 + 8 = 40 = L, and NUT_INSET_Y had already derived to exactly that 8 before anyone had seen the page.
NUT_PATTERN_Y24 mmvendor
used by NUT_INSET_Y, Z_NUT_X_2
24 - the nut’s bolt pattern along its 40 mm face, the short axis of the four tapped M5. SETTLED: CHALLENGED AND HELD, 2026-10-10. A caliper reading of 22 was taken off the part and then withdrawn as sloppy; the spec sheet’s 24 is the one. It is recorded because the 2 mm was not harmless: these four are THREADED INTO THE NUT and have no float, so the sole plate’s clearance holes have to match them exactly, and a 22 would have moved Sheet 4’s N1-N4 to X 29 and 51, Sheet 11’s to Y 66 and 88, and Sheet 5’s to plus or minus 11. OPEN: THIS NOTE USED TO READ “Pattern along Y” AND NOTHING ELSE, which is why the symbol drew suspicion the moment a second figure appeared - every other number in this group cites the DSG1605 page and this one cited nothing. A thin note is not evidence of a thin number, but it is what makes a good number impossible to defend.
NUT_AXIS_TO_FACE20 mmvendor
used by NUT_FACE_BELOW_BEAM, X_NUT_FACE_Z
source Vevor DSG1605 page, printed manual with the ball screw kit
20 - the screw axis above the ball nut housing’s mounting face, h on the DSG1605 page. FLAG: IT DISAGREES WITH THE SUPPORT BLOCKS BY 8, AND ONE OF THE TWO IS WRONG. The Y screw axis can be reached two ways and they do not meet. Via the blocks: BF12 sits upside down with its base level with the interposer's underside, so the axis is Y_BEAM_UNDERSIDE - T_INTERPOSER - SCREW_AXIS_H = 182 - 10 - 25 = 147. Via the nut: the face is NUT_FACE_BELOW_BEAM under the beam and the axis NUT_AXIS_TO_FACE above it, so 182 - 63 + 20 = 139. Those are the two ends of ONE screw. The same 8 is in the as-built pair as well, which points at a constant being wrong rather than a slip. SCREW_AXIS_H's 25 is confirmed off the BF/BK12 spec - his, 2026-10-10 - so the error is on the nut side: either NUT_FACE_BELOW_BEAM is 8 too big or NUT_AXIS_TO_FACE is 8 too small. OPEN: NOTHING HERE HAS BEEN CHANGED TO MAKE IT CLOSE. THE MEASUREMENT THAT SETTLES IT: the nut housing's H, across the mounting face - if it is 40 then h is 20 and NUT_FACE_BELOW_BEAM moves to 55; if it is 56 then h is 28 and the nut chain was right all along. Measuring the built machine's beam underside down to the nut's mounting face does it just as well. WHAT IT WOULD ACTUALLY MOVE, and it is less than it looks. dims.py deps lists 14 dependents including all four BLOCK_*_COL, and reading that as “the sixteen bearing block holes move” is WRONG - his correction, 2026-10-10. RAIL_LOW_Z and RAIL_HIGH_Z are beam-local, measured off the beam’s bottom face; RAIL_LOW_XEP and the BLOCK_*_COL only re-express those same holes as coordinates from the PLATE’S BOTTOM EDGE. Move that edge and the numbers change while every hole stays exactly where it was against the beam. The only thing genuinely at stake is XEP_H - how far the X end plate runs down - and running 8 longer than it needs to is spare metal, not a fault. So this is worth getting right, but it is not a reason to hold up Sheet 2a. The half-of-H reasoning below is the part most likely to be the fault: it assumes the page’s H is 40 and that the bore is centred across it, and his 2026-10-10 figure for the nut across is 52, not 40. If H is really 56 this symbol is 28. SETTLED: THE CONVENTION IS THE SAME ONE BK-12 AND BF-12 USE for their h of 25, which is what lets these numbers be added up the way they are. It checks against the page’s own H of 40: the bore sits centred across that width, so h is half of it. This is the last term in the height chain at both ends of the machine - on Y the nut hangs and the face points down, on X the screw runs on top of the beam and the face points up.
CASTING_AXIS_ABOVE_FOOT25 mmpart
used by X_NUT_FACE_Z
25 - the cast stepper frame’s screw axis above its own foot, measured 2026-10-02. OPEN: IT EXISTED ONLY IN y-beam-support.md PROSE UNTIL 2026-10-09 and now feeds the X nut height, which is why it is a symbol. The casting inherits BK12’s bearing geometry, so this is the same 25 as BK-12 and BF-12’s h - worth knowing as a cross-check, not a derivation, since nothing guarantees a casting copies a support block’s axis height.
X_BEAM_TOP_XC151 mmderived
built from X_BLOCK_UPPER_CENTRE + (BEAM_H - RAIL_HIGH_Z)
used by X_NUT_FACE_Z
source his, 2026-10-10
151 - the X beam’s top face above the BOTTOM of the X carriage plate. FLAG: IT WAS 167 AND CARRIED AS MEASURED UNTIL 2026-10-10, AND IT CANNOT EVER HAVE BEEN MEASURED: the X carriage plate has never been on the beam, so there was no such distance to put a rule across. His, 2026-10-10. It is DERIVED now and the chain is his: Sheet 3a puts the upper block row X4/8/12/16 at 152 up the plate, the Vevor block page puts 6 from a hole centre to the block’s side, so that side is at 158 - and the block stands 7 proud of the beam top, which is BLOCK_W/2 less the rail’s 15 from the beam’s top face. 158 less 7 is 151. The expression reaches the same point more directly, through the block CENTRE, which is the rail centre: X_BLOCK_UPPER_CENTRE plus the beam’s own 15 above that rail. SETTLED: THE LOWER ROW AGREES: X1/5/9/13 at 30 and X2/6/10/14 at 62 centre the lower block at 46, less the same 15 puts the beam’s BOTTOM at 31, and 31 plus BEAM_H is 151. OPEN: THE 16 IT TOOK OFF MOVES REAL HOLES - X_NUT_FACE_Z, X_DOUBLER_BOTTOM and X_DOUBLER_BOLT_XC all come down with it, so Sheet 3a’s back-face holes are 16 lower than they were drawn. IT IS REFERENCED TO THE CARRIAGE PLATE, NOT TO THE TORSION BOX, and that is deliberate: everything bolted to the carriage plate is laid out from that plate’s own bottom edge, so a height taken from the box would have to be converted every time and would go stale the moment the gantry moved. X_BEAM_TOP_Z is the box-referenced figure and the two are not interchangeable.
X_BLOCK_UPPER_CENTRE136 mmpinned
used by X_BEAM_TOP_XC, X_DOUBLER_BLOCK_CLEAR
136 - the upper pair of X-axis bearing blocks, centred between Sheet 3a's X1-X16 columns at 120 and 152. OPEN: THOSE TWO ARE 32 APART, WHICH IS BLOCK_PITCH_X, THE ACROSS-RAIL PITCH - not the along-rail 36. The X carriage plate hangs with its 407 VERTICAL, so the X beam's rails run along the plate's Y and each block extends BLOCK_W along the plate's X. Getting that backwards makes the block look 77 long up the plate instead of 44 wide, and it is an easy mistake: the Y values on that same sheet really are on the 36.
X_DOUBLER_BLOCK_CLEAR3 mmderived
built from X_DOUBLER_BOTTOM - X_BLOCK_UPPER_CENTRE - (BLOCK_W / 2)
3 - the gap up the plate between the upper X bearing blocks and the bottom of the nut doubler. They share the plate's REAR face, and nothing had checked they clear until the doubler was drawn on Sheet 3a on 2026-10-09. SETTLED: THE REASON THEY CLEAR IS STRUCTURAL, NOT ARITHMETIC - his, 2026-10-09: the doubler sits ABOVE THE TOP OF THE X BEAM, and the blocks ride the beam, so they cannot reach it. X_DOUBLER_BOTTOM less X_BEAM_TOP_XC is exactly T_INTERPOSER, 10, because the nut height is built up from the beam top through that interposer. The 3 is the slack that follows - it was 19 until X_BEAM_TOP_XC came down 16 on 2026-10-10, and 3 is tight enough to be worth knowing even though nothing depends on it. It is the number to re-read if the nut height ever changes - but the ordering is guaranteed by the chain, not by this value staying positive.
X_SCREW_AXIS60 mmderived
built from RAIL_STACK + (EXT_W / 2)
used by X_SOLE_BRACKET_L, X_NUT_BOLT_NEAR, X_NUT_BOLT_FAR
60 - the X screw axis out from the X carriage plate's REAR face, the face the doubler bolts to. SETTLED: THE PLATE'S OWN THICKNESS IS NOT IN THIS CHAIN and that is the whole difference from Y. SCREW_AXIS_X on the Y side is 72.19 because it measures from the X end plate's INNER face and so carries the plate's 12.19; here the root sits on the rear face and the 12.19 drops out. That is why every X number on Sheet 11 is round where Sheet 5's are not, and it also means T_X_CARRIAGE_PLATE - a nominal 12.7 still flagged to caliper - cannot move any of them.
X_SOLE_BRACKET_L86 mmderived
built from X_SCREW_AXIS + (NUT_FACE_X / 2)
used by SOLE_SAME_AS_X_CHECK
86 - the X nut sole plate's root to tip, which is Sheet 5's 98.19 less the 12.19 that the end plate contributed. SETTLED: IT CLOSES ON THE NUT: 86 less X_NUT_BOLT_FAR's 80 is 6, which is NUT_INSET_X, so the plate's tip finishes flush with the nut face's edge rather than at a number someone chose.
X_SOLE_ROOT_ROW6.095 mmderived
built from T_DOUBLER / 2
6.095 - the X sole plate's single root row, centred in the doubler's thickness. OPEN: IT IS THE SAME 6.095 SHEET 5 PUTS ITS *OTHER* ROW AT, and the coincidence is worth naming so nobody reads one as the other: on Y, 6.095 is centred in the END PLATE and 18.285 is centred in the doubler behind it. Here there is no end plate, the doubler is the first thing at the root, and 6.095 is the doubler's own mid-thickness.
X_NUT_BOLT_NEAR40 mmderived
built from X_SCREW_AXIS - (NUT_PATTERN_X / 2)
40 - the inboard of the two nut rows on the X sole plate.
X_NUT_BOLT_FAR80 mmderived
built from X_SCREW_AXIS + (NUT_PATTERN_X / 2)
80 - the outboard nut row. FLAG: THESE TWO BOLT INTO THE NUT'S OWN THREADS, so unlike the root row a ø5.5 clearance hole does not rescue a wrong position - the nut has no float at all.
X_DOUBLER_BOLT_XC186 mmderived
built from X_DOUBLER_BOTTOM + DOUBLER_BOLT_X
186 - the X doubler's bolt column where it lands ON THE X CARRIAGE PLATE, measured from that plate's bottom edge, which is Sheet 3a's X 0. It is the doubler's own DOUBLER_BOLT_X of 25 carried up to where the block sits. FLAG: SHEET 3a HAS TO GAIN FOUR HOLES FOR IT - the carriage plate exists, dyed and undrilled, and nothing in its schedule put a doubler on it until 2026-10-09. The Y side has the same column at the same 25 within the block, which is why DOUBLER_BOLT_X is shared rather than duplicated.
X_NUT_FACE_Z206 mmderived
built from X_BEAM_TOP_XC + T_INTERPOSER + CASTING_AXIS_ABOVE_FOOT + NUT_AXIS_TO_FACE
used by X_DOUBLER_BOTTOM
206 - the X ball nut’s mounting face, above the bottom of the X carriage plate. The chain is the beam top, the interposer the casting sits on, the casting’s own axis height, then the nut’s h. SETTLED: EVERY TERM COMES FROM A PART OR A PAGE: 151 from Sheet 3a’s own block rows, 10 the interposer he bought, 25 measured off the casting, 20 the DSG1605 page. OPEN: IT WAS 222 UNTIL 2026-10-10, on an X_BEAM_TOP_XC of 167 that could never have been measured. On X the face points UP because the screw runs on top of the beam - the mirror of Y, where the nut hangs and its face points down.
X_DOUBLER_BOTTOM161 mmderived
built from X_NUT_FACE_Z - DOUBLER_H
used by X_DOUBLER_BLOCK_CLEAR, X_DOUBLER_BOLT_XC
161 - where the X doubler’s BOTTOM edge sits on the back of the X carriage plate. Its TOP edge is X_NUT_FACE_Z, carrying four M5 tapped into that top edge for the X nut sole plate, the way Sheet 2d’s R1-R4 tap into that block’s end face. FLAG: THE BOTTOM EDGE IS WHAT GETS DIMENSIONED AND THE TOP IS WHERE THE WORK IS - read them the wrong way round and the part sits DOUBLER_H low with the sole plate missing the nut entirely. SETTLED: His reason for putting the sole plate on top: the bolts then come from ABOVE, where they stay reachable during assembly. The same instinct that inverted the Y nut.
NUT_SFU_PATTERN38 mmvendor
source Vevor DSG1605 page, printed manual with the ball screw kit
ø38 - the bolt circle of the SIX M5 that hold the DSG housing to the SFU ball nut’s own flange. FLAG: IT IS INTERNAL TO THE SCREW ASSEMBLY AND NOTHING ON THIS MACHINE BOLTS TO IT. The symbol exists only so the next person does not re-open the question that cost a round trip on 2026-10-09: the DSG1605 page shows 6-Y on this ø38 in its middle view AND a four-hole P 40 by C2 24 pattern on the flat in its left view, and the two look contradictory until you see they are different joints. The machine interface is the four - NUT_PATTERN_X and NUT_PATTERN_Y.
NUT_INSET_X6 mmderived

NUT_BOLT_X_NEAR40 mmderived

NUT_BOLT_X_FAR80 mmderived

SOLE_REACH73.81 mmderived

Free span of the sole bracket, doubler's outer face to the nut face's outboard edge
NUT_AIR_MIN20 mmpart
REQUIRED minimum clear air between the doubler's outer face and the ball nut's side. His number, 2026-10-03, off the Y beam build.
NUT_AIR_CLEAR21.81 mmderived

Actual clear air, doubler's outer face to the nut's near edge. Comes out 21.81, so it clears NUT_AIR_MIN by 1.81. If this ever drops below NUT_AIR_MIN the stack has moved.
SOLE_BRACKET_L86 mmderived
built from (SCREW_AXIS_X + (NUT_FACE_X / 2)) - T_X_ENDPLATE

86 - sole bracket root to tip, from the plate's OUTER face, which is where the doubler starts and so where the bracket's root is. The base lands on the doubler's end face alone and the plate carries on past it by XEP_SOLE_TAB to back it. FLAG: THE BASE IS INSIDE THIS LENGTH, not added to it.
SOLE_SAME_AS_X_CHECK0 mmderived
built from SOLE_BRACKET_L - X_SOLE_BRACKET_L
0 - and it must stay 0: the Y sole bracket and the X sole plate are ONE PART in three copies. NOT an alias on purpose - each keeps its own chain, the Y off SCREW_AXIS_X less the plate and the X off X_SCREW_AXIS, so this proves they land on one number rather than asserting it. They do because SCREW_AXIS_X less T_X_ENDPLATE IS X_SCREW_AXIS: both nuts stand the same distance out from the face the doubler bolts to. The root row and both nut rows coincide for the same reason.
ROOT_BOLT_X_ENDPLATE6.095 mmderivedretired
built from T_X_ENDPLATE / 2
RETIRED. The sole bracket's second root row, into the END PLATE's end face. The sole plate bolts into the doubler's edge only now, and XEP_SOLE_TAB backs the base instead. Kept because a retired symbol is the record that the row existed.
ROOT_BOLT_X_DOUBLER6.095 mmderived
built from T_DOUBLER / 2
6.095 - the sole bracket's single root row, from the bracket's root, centred in the doubler's thickness. FORCED, not chosen: a row off its plate's mid-thickness breaks out of it. These are the only edge holes that need the mill - Sheet 2 carries them as B1-B4.
ROOT_BOLT_Y_FIRST32 mmpinned
First of four Y stations across the 154 root, settled 2026-10-03. D1-D4, R1-R4 and R5-R8 all sit on the same stations, so one spread serves the doubler bolts and both sole-bracket rows.
ROOT_BOLT_Y_PITCH30 mmpinned
Pitch of those four stations: 32, 62, 92, 122. Leaves 32 of edge at each end of the 154.
THRUST_ARM60 mmderived

Drive thrust outboard of the plate's OUTER face. This is what the doubler and the two bolt rows are for.
DOUBLER_SEATING24.38 mmderived

Seating width at the plate's bottom with the doubler on
DOUBLER_H45 mmpinned
used by X_DOUBLER_BOTTOM, DOUBLER_BLOCK_CLEAR, XEP_WASTE_SPARE
Doubler height. 45, so it comes out of OFFCUTS - his reason, and the only one it needs: nothing requires a particular height here. THE CONSTRAINT IS AN UPPER BOUND, NOT A FIT: the doubler shares a face with the LOWER Y BEARING BLOCK ROW, and anything stopping below that row's bottom edge clears. DOUBLER_BLOCK_CLEAR is that gap, and nothing bears across it. 42 would still come out of the same offcut if more margin is wanted. NOT gated on the Y beam underside - the doubler sits ~17 inboard of the beam's inside face.
DOUBLER_BOTTOM_X10 mmalias
another name for XEP_SOLE_TAB
used by DOUBLER_BLOCK_CLEAR, DOUBLER_BOLT_X_PLATE
The doubler's end face in the END PLATE's machining frame. That face is level with the nut's mounting face and the plate reaches XEP_SOLE_TAB past it, so the doubler starts that far up the plate rather than at the plate's own end. Sheet 2a's B row bores in from here, not from X 0.
BOLT_SOLE_ROOT16 mmalias
another name for BOLT_Z_NUT
used by SOLE_ROOT_ENGAGE
M5 x 16 through the sole plate into the doubler's end edge - R1-R4, Sheet 2a's B1-B4. THE SAME BOLT AS THE FOUR NUTS, which is the point: one length and one tap setting cover every bolt in this joint.
SOLE_ROOT_ENGAGE9.65 mmderived
built from BOLT_SOLE_ROOT - T_SOLE_BRACKET
9.65 into the doubler's edge, 1.93 x D in 6061. The bolt crosses the sole plate and meets thread immediately - nothing sits between them.
SOLE_ROOT_TAP_DEPTH14 mmpinned
used by SOLE_ROOT_WALL
14 - how deep R1-R4 are drilled into the doubler's end face. FLAG: THE LIMIT IS NOT STRENGTH, IT IS THE D BOLT COMING THE OTHER WAY. D1-D4 sit at DOUBLER_BOLT_X on the SAME four Y stations, so each of these bores straight at one of them and has to stop short - SOLE_ROOT_WALL is what is left. SOLE_ROOT_ENGAGE needs 9.65 of thread, so 14 covers it with runout and drill point to spare and still leaves a wall worth having.
SOLE_ROOT_WALL8.5 mmderived
built from (DOUBLER_BOLT_X - (M5_D / 2)) - SOLE_ROOT_TAP_DEPTH
8.5 of aluminium between the bottom of an R hole and the side of the D bolt it points at. FLAG: IT MUST NOT GO NEGATIVE OR NEAR IT - break through and the D bolt's thread is ruined from a hole that is not even load-bearing. Measured to the bolt's side, M5_D/2 in from its centreline, not to the centreline.
DOUBLER_BLOCK_CLEAR3 mmderived
built from (RAIL_LOW_XEP - (BLOCK_W / 2)) - (DOUBLER_BOTTOM_X + DOUBLER_H)
3 - doubler top to the LOWER Y bearing block row's bottom edge, both in the end plate's frame. FLAG: IT MUST NOT GO NEGATIVE. Negative means the doubler holds a block off its mounting face, which is the one surface on this plate that has to be flat. XEP_SOLE_TAB does not move it - the doubler and the block row both shift up by it.

the front fin

FIN_STOCK301.625 mmalias
another name for PLATE_H
Each front fin's stock height, 301.625 - PLATE_H, because the fin stands as high as the other four plates and is dimensioned off the same bottom edge. It is 11 7/8in, his piece 2026-10-07; it had been carried as a measured 302 and it is not 302, nor the nominal 12in it was already corrected away from once. OPEN: THE NAME SAYS SQUARE AND IT IS NO LONGER ONE: both fins came out of a SINGLE 301.625 square on one diagonal cut until 2026-10-08, when he put each fin on its OWN piece of 10 mm plate. That is what released FIN_TOP_W back to a chosen 100 and retired FIN_KERF - top plus base plus kerf no longer has to close on anything. Kept under this name because every sheet and both documents refer to it.
FIN_KERF2 mmnominalretired
FLAG: RETIRED 2026-10-08 AND NOTHING DERIVES FROM IT. It was the bandsaw kerf of the single diagonal cut that nested both fins out of one 301.625 square. He put each fin on its own piece of plate instead, so there is no cut to allow for and FIN_TOP_W stopped being forced by it. Kept rather than deleted because it is the reason FIN_TOP_W reads 100 again rather than 99.625, and a bare deletion would leave that reversal with no record.
FIN_BASE_W200 mmpart
THE BASE IS THE REFERENCE, his call 2026-10-07 - so it is a fixed 200 and the top absorbs whatever the square leaves. The base is the end that bolts down and bears, which is why it gets to be the round number. It was the other way round until the square turned out to be 301.625 rather than 302, and then the shortfall was landing on the bearing end.
FIN_TOP_W100 mmpart
100, his choice, and it is CHOSEN AGAIN rather than derived - 2026-10-08. It was forced to 99.625 for as long as both fins nested out of one 301.625 square, because top plus base plus kerf had to close on the square or the two halves did not fit. Each fin is its own piece of plate now, so nothing couples the top width to the base width and the round number comes back. FIN_BASE_W stays the reference end at 200 for the reason it always was: it is the end that bolts down and bears.
FIN_COL_OUTB35 mmpinned
used by FIN_COL_INB, FIN_OVERHANG
Outboard M8 column from the fin's outboard factory edge. CORRECTED from 20 - the 1.8 mm proud figure was computed off that dead value.
FIN_COL_INB65 mmderived
built from FIN_COL_OUTB + (EXT_W / 2)
Inboard M8 column. 65.
FIN_OVERHANG20 mmderived
built from FIN_COL_OUTB - EXT_BORE_INSET
used by FIN_PROUD
Fin overhanging the Y beam's outboard face
FIN_PROUD16.61 mmderived
built from FIN_OVERHANG - T_SIDE_PLATE
used by FIN_COVERS_TONGUE_END, FIN_ANGLE_T1_END
Fin proud of the side plate's outer face. WAS RECORDED AS 1.8 off the dead FIN_COL_OUTB of 20. The side TONGUE must terminate short of the riser's station - it would otherwise be held out by this 16.825 rather than seating on the side plate. The word 'curb' is retired as of 2026-10-04: it implied a face the plate drops in against, and there is no channel - the plate stands on the top skin and the tongue laps it from outboard.
WINDOW_W60.5 mmpinned
source machine/lateral-stiffness.md; machine/y-beam-support.md
Clearance window for the cast stepper frame, in each front fin. 60.5 OVER A 60 CASTING - half a millimetre total, a slip fit and deliberately little, because the window's loose axis is its HEIGHT, not its width. Corrected from 62 on 2026-10-03 by tools/audit-prose.py: nine places in the prose said 60.5 and gave the reasoning, the registry said 62 with a one-line note and no source, and nothing had ever compared them.
WINDOW_H55 mmpinned
used by WINDOW_CLEAR_TOTAL, WINDOW_BOTTOM_Y
Height of the window's full-width opening, measured DOWN from the Y beam's underside. His, and the loose axis - it is what the casting's clearance consumes, which is why the width is allowed to be a 0.5 slip fit. FLAG: THE WINDOW IS IN THE REAR PLATE, NOT THE FINS - the fins are solid and BF12 face-mounts into them instead. FLAG: THE OPENING IS NOT THE WHOLE CLEARANCE - PROBE_NOTCH_H carries on below it and WINDOW_CLEAR_TOTAL is the figure that matters to the probe.
PROBE_NOTCH_W25 mmpinned
25 - width of the notch that runs on below the window's full-width opening, centred on the window. His. It clears the back of the proximity probe body, which is narrower than the casting the opening above it is cut for.
PROBE_NOTCH_H30 mmpinned
used by WINDOW_CLEAR_TOTAL
30 - how far the notch runs on past WINDOW_H. His. Two things need it: the back of the proximity probe body, and getting the casting and interposer in from BEHIND the machine - they slide through the window BK12 end first, which this is what makes possible.
PROBE_NOTCH_R6 mmpinned
6 - radius at all four of the notch's corners, the two at its mouth as well as the two at its bottom. His. FLAG: THE BOTTOM PAIR IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS. There the metal wraps around the end of the notch, which is the stress raiser - this plate is the rear shear panel and that is where a crack would start. The pair at the mouth is a protruding corner of metal rather than a notch tip, so rounding it is tidiness. It is deliberately smaller than WINDOW_CORNER_R: a 10 in a 25-wide notch leaves only 5 of flat across its bottom, where this leaves 13.
WINDOW_TOP_OVER0.5 mmpinned
used by WINDOW_TOP_Y
source his, 2026-10-17
0.5 - how far the window's top edge is cut ABOVE the Y beam's underside. His. FLAG: IT IS AN OVERCUT ON PURPOSE AND MUST NOT GO TO ZERO. Cut level with the underside, any tolerance in the cut or in the plate's placement leaves a tongue of steel hanging below the beam's bottom face, where the casting and the interposer have to pass. Over-cutting costs nothing: the metal above the window is backed by the beam itself.
WINDOW_TOP_Y182.5 mmderived
built from Y_BEAM_UNDERSIDE + WINDOW_TOP_OVER
used by WINDOW_H_CUT
182.5 - the window's top edge in the rear plate's own frame, WINDOW_TOP_OVER proud of the beam's underside so no steel can stand below it. Beam-referenced, so nothing about the delivered plate moves it.
WINDOW_H_CUT55.5 mmderived
built from WINDOW_TOP_Y - WINDOW_BOTTOM_Y
55.5 - the full-width opening's height AS CUT, top edge to bottom edge. It is not WINDOW_H: that is the clearance measured down from the beam's underside, and this is the same opening plus the overcut above it. The shop cuts this one.
WINDOW_CLEAR_TOTAL85 mmderived
built from WINDOW_H + PROBE_NOTCH_H
used by PROBE_NOTCH_BOTTOM_Y
85 below the Y beam's underside, the full depth of clearance at each window. This is the figure the probe body and the slide-in assembly are measured against, not WINDOW_H on its own.
WINDOW_BOTTOM_Y127 mmderived
built from Y_BEAM_UNDERSIDE - WINDOW_H

Z carriage

SPACER_SKIM1 mmpinned
used by NUT_SHIM
1.0, THE AMOUNT ACTUALLY CUT - and it is deliberately LESS than the 1.3 step it is closing. FLAG: THE SKIM HAS TWO JOBS AND ONLY ONE OF THEM NEEDS A SKIM. Facing both spacers IN ONE SETUP is the only way to make the pair coplanar and flat, and that is what this cut is for. Closing the gap to the ball nut is the other job, and a shim does it better - see NUT_SHIM. It used to be 1.0 described as the whole step, which made a one-shot cut on the mill trip carry a number measured to about two tenths.

OPEN: 15.2 IS A PLACEHOLDER so the arithmetic resolves - it is the previous bar's micrometer reading less the skim, and the new 160 x 50 stock is a different bar. MIC THE FACED PAIR AND PUT THAT NUMBER HERE, with provenance measured. It feeds Z_BOLT_ENGAGE, which is bounded at both ends by the block's 8 mm tapped depth, and SPINDLE_OFFSET - so both are provisional until it is real. This part has been wrong twice already, once as a fraction (5/8in, 15.875) and once by instrument (16.41 on calipers against 16.22 on a micrometer), which is why it is not being guessed a third time.

SHELF_BAR_BOTTOM-110.8 mmderived
built from SHELF_BAR_TOP - SHELF_BAR_H
used by SHELF_BOLT_LOW_Z, SHELF_BOLT_HIGH_Z
SHELF_BOLT_LOW_Z-95.8 mmderived
built from SHELF_BAR_BOTTOM + SHELF_HORIZ_Z_LOW
used by SHELF_BOLT_LOW_X
Machine Z of the lower bolt. Near the lower bearing block row - hence the blind tap.
SHELF_HORIZ_CLEARANCE8.4 mmpinned
used by SHELF_VERT_CLEARANCE
8.4 - the clearance hole through the bar for the two HORIZONTAL M8, the standard close fit, NOT the BEAM_BOLT_CLEARANCE 9 used everywhere a plate meets extrusion. SETTLED: THE BLOCK MOUNTING IS THE ONE JOINT THAT MUST NOT BE SLOPPY, which is the argument tramming.md already made for shimming the shelf over oversizing the bearing block holes: the shim is the adjustment, so the bar is the platform. The bar carries the gantry's share into the plate by friction and bolt shear, so if that joint slips the bar drops by the RADIAL clearance - 0.5 at 9, the whole roll budget, against 0.2 here. This costs no adjustment range: the shim goes on TOP of the bar, so these two bolts do not move when roll is set. Going further to 8.2 buys 0.1 and leaves the bar's two holes 0.2 total to agree with the plate's two blind taps across the 20 of SHELF_Z_COUPLE - a bind risk on a manual mill, and a forced bolt is the worse joint. TRANSFER-DRILL: the bar's holes are through and the plate's are blind, so drill the bar first and use it to spot the plate.
SHELF_VERT_CLEARANCE8.4 mmalias
another name for SHELF_HORIZ_CLEARANCE
Same close fit for the two VERTICAL M8, and for the same reason read the other way round: these pass through the shim plane and get SLACKENED to slide a shim in, so a free-fit hole would let the bar shift in Y on re-torque and walk off the T-nuts at SHELF_VERT_REAR and SHELF_VERT_FRONT. The counterbore above it is M8_SOCKET_CBORE deep and its DIAMETER is still open - see SHELF_BAR_L, which it gates.
SHELF_SHIM_W25.4 mmalias
another name for SHELF_BAR_W
The 1in shim strip on hand is exactly the bar's width, so a strip cut to SHELF_BAR_L is a full-face shim. Slot it open at Y 15 and Y 45 to clear the vertical M8.
BEAM_BOLT_CLEARANCE9 mmpinned
used by ROLL_RANGE
9 mm clearance on M8 where a plate meets extrusion - the repo's deliberate figure, the joint working by friction
ROLL_RANGE0.5 mmderived
built from (BEAM_BOLT_CLEARANCE - 8) / 2
used by ROLL_RANGE_PER_100
Vertical float of the beam in its own bolt holes, and therefore the CEILING on shim-based roll - about 0.5. Caps the adjustment; the shim stack does not.
BEAM_LEN1000 mmpart
used by ROLL_RANGE_PER_100, Y_BEAM_GAP, BOX_FRONT_OVERHANG, SIDE_TONGUE_L, TRAVEL_RAIL_LIMITED, RAIL_CBORE_N_BEAM, TT_BED_FORE_AFT_CHECK
Span between the two shelves, so the lever the roll shim works over
ROLL_RANGE_PER_1000.05 mmderived
built from ROLL_RANGE / BEAM_LEN * 100
Correctable tram error at a 100 mm circle. A 0.03 shim, the finest on hand, is worth 0.003 at the same circle.

BF12_ROW1_Y165 mmderived

used by BF12_ROW2_Y
165, and FLAG: IT IS BEAM-REFERENCED NOW rather than converted from a plate edge - his chain, 2026-10-08, and it closes the open item that two earlier commits could not. BF12 sits upside down with its base face LEVEL WITH THE UNDERSIDE OF THE INTERPOSER PLATE, because at the far end of the beam the stepper frame / BK12 casting hangs below that same interposer, and the interposer bolts to the Y beam's underside. So the screw's two ends are set by one surface and the row is Y_BEAM_UNDERSIDE less the interposer less the casting's own 7. source his, 2026-10-17
75 - first of eight torsion-box bolts, then XBOX_PITCH. Stations: 75, 225, 375, 525, 675, 825, 975, 1125. FLAG: IT IS NOT CENTRED ON THE PLATE AND MUST NOT BE. Centring reads (REAR_PLATE_L less seven pitches) / 2, which ties all eight to a sheared plate edge and to an envelope figure that is not the delivered plate. The eight span 75 to 1125, leaving 85.47 to the far end of the 1210.47 plate. The side plates run 150 from 50 - a different tongue, and footnote 8.3 is theirs. Note 7.8 is why the exact stations cost nothing anyway - these are drill-guide holes with no mating pattern to miss.

the BF12 tongue, Sheet 2b

XEP_STOCK_L300 mmmeasured
used by XEP_WASTE_L, TONGUE_TOP
The 1/2in plate stock for the X end plates: 200 x 300, his. THE LENGTH THE RIGHT-HAND PLATE ENDS AT, because its far end is not cut - see TONGUE_TOP. FLAG: WAS RECORDED AS 304.5 AND THAT WAS WRONG: a 304.5 x 154 piece was written down as the stock, the tongue was built on it, and the real stock is 200 x 300. Every number on Sheet 2b past X 242.5 moved when it was corrected. It is the repo's own README warning twice over - a stock dimension taken from the conversation rather than from the part, and then derived from.
XEP_WASTE_L55 mmderived
built from XEP_STOCK_L - XEP_CUT
used by XEP_WASTE_SPARE
55 - what is left of the 300 past the LEFT plate's end, running the full XEP_STOCK_W. The third doubler is cut from it, turned so its 154 runs across the width and its DOUBLER_H along this. FLAG: IT MUST STAY AT OR ABOVE DOUBLER_H plus a kerf either side, or the third doubler needs its own piece of plate. It shrinks if the plate is ever cut longer.
XEP_WASTE_SPARE10 mmderived
built from XEP_WASTE_L - DOUBLER_H
10 across, what is left after the third doubler is taken out of the waste. The kerf comes out of this, so it is the margin on that nest rather than free metal.
XEP_STOCK_W200 mmmeasured
Width of the same stock. The plate is XEP_W (154) wide, so cutting one out leaves a 45 strip - and THAT is where DOUBLER_H's 45 comes from. The doubler is the offcut, which is the whole reason it is 45 rather than a round 50. SETTLED: ALL THREE DOUBLERS COME OUT OF THESE TWO PIECES - his. One strip per piece gives two, and the LEFT plate is cut to XEP_CUT out of the 300, so XEP_WASTE_L x this width is left past its end - the third, the X carriage's, is turned 90 degrees into that. Nothing has to be bought for it.
TONGUE_TOP300 mmalias
another name for XEP_STOCK_L
used by TONGUE_H, TONGUE_EDGE_MARGIN
Far end of the RIGHT-HAND plate - the stock's own end, uncut, so it is XEP_STOCK_L and not a chosen length. His reason was 'one less cut': leave the far end alone and let the corner cut make the tongue. The left-hand plate still stops at XEP_H, which is the single thing making the pair non-interchangeable.
TONGUE_H55 mmderived
built from TONGUE_TOP - XEP_CUT
used by TONGUE_CLEAR
BF12 tongue length past the 2a outline - 65, DERIVED from the stock rather than chosen, and it only has to beat TONGUE_MIN. It has been 40, then 50, then briefly 61.975 on a stock length that turned out to be wrong, then 57.475, and it is now whatever XEP_STOCK_L leaves above XEP_CUT. SETTLED: IT IS A ROUND 65 AND THAT IS NOT A ROUNDING: 300 less 235, both exact. The old warning here said never to print a round number for it, because '60 x 60' had once been printed while the real figure was 61.975 - that warning was about a rounded figure, not about this one. The sheet dimensions the stock length and the corner cut; the tongue has no cut-to length of its own. THE BF12 ROWS NEVER MOVED FOR ANY OF IT - their X is TONGUE_BF12_ROW1/ROW2, set by the beam top plus the interposer, so this symbol follows them and never the reverse.
TONGUE_BF12_ROW1_BARE252 mmderived
built from XEP_CUT + BF12_BASE_TO_ROW1
used by TONGUE_BF12_ROW1
First tongue BF12 row BEFORE the interposer: 242, which is 7 above the TOP OF THE X BEAM, matching Sheet 7's own pre-interposer offset. OPEN: IT WAS A FLAT 249.5 AND IS AN EXPRESSION NOW, because the beam top sits at XEP_CUT up this plate and that moved to 235 on 2026-10-10 - the row has to follow the cut or the sheet and the registry drift apart again. The reference is the beam top, not the plate end - XEP_TOP_ABOVE_BEAM makes those the same point on this plate, which is easy to read as a coincidence and is not one.
TONGUE_BF12_ROW1262 mmderived
built from TONGUE_BF12_ROW1_BARE + T_INTERPOSER
used by TONGUE_BF12_ROW2
THE INTERPOSER GOES UNDER THE CAST STEPPER FRAME - beam top, interposer, casting - so the frame and the screw it carries both stand 9.525 above the beam top, and these holes must be drilled where the screw ACTUALLY ends up rather than where a casting sitting straight on the beam would put it. The X stepper end has one too - there are three, Y1, Y2 and X. The X tongue is ABOVE its beam where the Y frames hang BELOW theirs, so the interposer lifts this end rather than dropping it; on each sheet's own frame that is still an addition. Same correction as BF12_ROW1_Y on Sheet 7.
TONGUE_BF12_ROW2280 mmderived
built from TONGUE_BF12_ROW1 + BF12_ROW_PITCH
used by TONGUE_EDGE_MARGIN
Second tongue row, same two columns at Y 7 and 53.
TONGUE_EDGE_MARGIN20 mmderived
built from TONGUE_TOP - TONGUE_BF12_ROW2
Far BF12 row to the tongue's end - 30 on the 300 stock. Must stay well clear of an M5; 5.5 at the original 40 was not, which drove the tongue to 50 and then to the full piece. DERIVED, so it moves when the stock does and never sets anything.
CORNER_CUT_Y60 mmalias
another name for EXT_W
Where the corner cut starts up the far end of both X end plates - 60, and it is EXT_W because that is the beam's footprint off the back edge. Below it the plate must stay full width to cover the upper extrusion's end; above it there is nothing but plate. His cut, 2026-10-04.
CORNER_CUT_ABOVE_NUT188 mmpinned
used by CORNER_CUT_X_END
Where the corner cut lands on the front edge, measured from the NUT PLANE rather than from the plate's bottom edge. FLAG: THE NUT PLANE IS THE RIGHT REFERENCE because the thing this clearance is against - the far-upper bearing block - is fixed to the beam, and so is the plane. Pinned where it is so the cut stops crowding that block.
CORNER_CUT_X_END198 mmderived
built from XEP_SOLE_TAB + CORNER_CUT_ABOVE_NUT
Where the corner cut lands on the plate's FRONT edge, at Y = XEP_W, in each plate's own machining frame. The same number on BOTH plates - 2a cuts from (XEP_CUT, CORNER_CUT_Y) and 2b from (XEP_STOCK_L, CORNER_CUT_Y), and they meet here. ON 2B THE CUT REPLACES THE STEP rather than following it: one diagonal instead of cutting along X and then across Y, which is the 'one less cut' that lets the far end stay uncut. CHECKED, computed not measured: the nearest feature is the far-upper bearing block M5 at (BLOCK_HIGH_COL_B, 133.5), and the perpendicular distance to the cut is 19.9 mm on 2a and 23.4 on 2b, so a ~10 mm counterbore leaves about 15 mm of material on the tighter of the two. FLAG: THE CUT AND THAT BLOCK MOVE TOGETHER, both carrying XEP_SOLE_TAB, which is what keeps those two figures true.
BF12_H43 mmpart
43 - the height of BF12’s CENTRE SECTION, the part that holds the bearing. FLAG: IT IS NOT THE HEIGHT OF THE WHOLE BLOCK and it used to be described as if it were, which is what gave the tongue a floor 13 too high. His, 2026-10-10: BF12 is a tall centre boss with a LOW WING either side, and the four mounting holes are in the wings, which are BF12_WING_H at 30. So the part that has to be backed by anything is 30 tall; the 43 boss stands above it carrying the bearing and touches nothing. See TONGUE_MIN, which now runs on the wings.
TONGUE_MIN40 mmderived
built from T_INTERPOSER + BF12_WING_H
used by TONGUE_CLEAR
FLAG: THE TONGUE’S LOWER BOUND, and it came down from 53 to 40 on 2026-10-10. It used to read BF12_H + T_INTERPOSER on the reasoning that the tongue must back the WHOLE of BF12. His correction: it only has to reach both rows of mounting bolts, which is BF12_WING_H’s 30 - the two low side wings the bolts actually go into - not the 43 of the centre boss, which carries the bearing and touches nothing. The block can stand proud of the tongue above its top row and nothing cares. SETTLED: That takes TONGUE_CLEAR from 4 to 17, so the tongue stops being a tight constraint - and the old 53 was the number that made an 8 mm change elsewhere look like it would break this.
TONGUE_CLEAR15 mmderived
built from TONGUE_H - TONGUE_MIN
How much tongue there is past the floor BF12 needs - 25 on the 200 x 300 stock, which went from a nervous 4.95 to this when TONGUE_MIN came down to 40 and XEP_CUT to 235. THE CHECK TO RUN BEFORE SUBSTITUTING A DIFFERENT PIECE OF PLATE: any stock whose length leaves this positive will do, so the minimum usable plate is XEP_H + TONGUE_MIN, about 295. The 300 in hand clears by about 5.

the torsion box

T_BB_SKIN_TOP24 mmmeasured
used by BOX_THK, FIN_ANGLE_BOLT_DEAD
source His micrometer on the Crosscut Hardwoods sheet, 2026-10-13 - sold as 24, verified to 0.005
24 - the TOP skin, and the thicker of the two. SETTLED: MEASURED ON THE SHEET IN HAND 2026-10-13 - his micrometer, sold as 24, and the registry carried 25 as a nominal until the board was bought. The 1 it lost is the whole of the change; the DECISION is still 'the thicker sheet goes on top'. SETTLED: HIS CALL 2026-10-11, and torsion-box.md had already named it the one place the thick sheet earns its place: the top skin takes the riser feet, the Y plate bolt line and the five standing plates' bottom edges IN BEARING. FLAG: IT IS NOT FOR STIFFNESS - the thick sheet on both skins buys +46% against +100% for thin skins on 150 ribs, and most of that 46% is only the box getting deeper. ASYMMETRIC SKINS ARE FINE IN A TORSION BOX, which is what lets the material go where the bearing is. NOTE: THE UNDERSIDE OF THE TOP SKIN DOES NOT MOVE: it is set by the post ledge, the bottom skin and the cavity, so this only makes the box thicker and stands the machine that much higher off the floor. Z 0 is the top skin, so nothing above the box moves at all. NOTE: THE RIB NOTCHES ARE CUT TO THIS, and it is now the sheet's own figure rather than a catalogue one.
T_BB_SKIN_BOT18 mmmeasured
used by BOX_THK
source His micrometer on the Crosscut Hardwoods sheet, 2026-10-13 - sold as 18, verified to 0.005
18 - the BOTTOM skin, the thinner sheet, and it stayed the thinner one when the top went to the thick sheet on 2026-10-11. Nothing bears on it: it rests on the post ledges and works purely as the lower flange, where separation squared does the work rather than thickness. SETTLED: MEASURED 2026-10-13 - his micrometer, sold as 18, where the registry had carried 19 as a nominal.
BOX_RIB_H100 mmpinned
used by SAND_PORT_PLUG_LOAD, BOX_THK
Egg-crate rib height between the skins. His, 2026-10-03.

the sand ports, one per bay

SAND_PORT_D25.4 mmpinned
used by SAND_PORT_PLUG_LOAD
25.4 - 1in, the sand port through each skin, the same size in both. His, 2026-10-14. SETTLED: CENTRED IN THE BAY, and the centring is not for looks: dry sand stands at about a 34 degree repose angle, so from a centre hole the drain crater reaches roughly BOX_RIB_H / tan 34, about 150 - more than the 110 half-bay - and the bay empties to a shallow corner residue. A port in a corner would strand a wedge.
SAND_PORT_PLUG_LOAD0.0760 kgderived
built from (BOX_RIB_H / 1000) * 1.5 * (3.14159 * (SAND_PORT_D / 2) ** 2) / 1000
0.076 - the weight of sand bearing on one plug, in KILOGRAMS: a BOX_RIB_H column of sand at about 1.5 g/cc over the port's own area. SETTLED: IT IS THE WHOLE REASON THE PLUG WORKS FROM BELOW - 76 grams is nothing against the friction of a rubber plug in a hole, so the plug does not need a taper seated from above, a chain, or any access from the top. Fit it FROM BELOW with a pull tab underneath: pull it out to drain, push it back in to seal. NOTE: THE UNIT IS kg AND NOT mm, the only symbol in this file that is not a length - it is here because a design decision rests on it and the arithmetic should not have to be redone to check it.
SAND_PORT_DOWEL12.7 mmnominalretired
FLAG: RETIRED 2026-10-14 AND NOTHING USES IT. It was the dowel that pushed a cork back into the bottom skin FROM ABOVE, in the scheme where the cork was pushed up into the bay to drain and had to be reseated through the top port. The plug is fitted from BELOW now - see SAND_PORT_PLUG_LOAD - so there is nothing to reach down and do. Kept because it is the reason the top ports stopped being needed for anything but filling, which is what made the captured bed survivable.
SAND_PORT_CHAIN_L152.4 mmnominalretired
FLAG: RETIRED 2026-10-14 AND NOTHING USES IT. 6in of chain on each cork, whose job was to find the cork again after it was pushed up into the bay, fished with a magnet through the top port. The plug comes DOWNWARD out of the hole now and never enters the bay, so there is nothing to find. Kept because the chain is what the fill procedure was written around, and a bare deletion would leave that reversal with no record.
T_APRON_SKIN6.35 mmpinned
6.35 - 1/4in Baltic birch laid over the part of the top skin the tee track bed does not cover, held down with DOUBLE-SIDED TAPE. His, 2026-10-14, and what it is for is the FIVE FRONT-ROW SAND PORTS: the bed runs to 1006.35 and the front row of ports is centred at 1081.5, so those five come out on the open apron where the toolsetter and his storage live. SETTLED: TAPE AND NOT GLUE OR SCREWS - it is a cover, not structure, and it has to come off the day a port is used. NOTE: IT IS ALSO A SACRIFICIAL APRON SURFACE, which the bare top skin was not.
SAND_PORT_FIRST129.5 mmderived
built from T_BOX_WALL_WEB + BOX_BAY_W_END / 2
129.5 - the first port's centre, in from the box's edge: the perimeter web plus half an end bay. The box is square and the grid is the same both ways, so this is the same figure in X and Y. The remaining centres step by SAND_PORT_STEP_END then SAND_PORT_STEP, and tools/make-box-doc.py prints the whole row.
SAND_PORT_STEP_END239 mmderived
built from BOX_BAY_W_END / 2 + T_BB_RIB + BOX_BAY_W / 2
239 - from the first port to the second, which crosses a rib between an END bay and an inner one. The two steps at the ends of the row are this and the three in the middle are SAND_PORT_STEP.
SAND_PORT_STEP237 mmderived
built from BOX_BAY_W + T_BB_RIB
237 - from one port to the next between two INNER bays. Five ports a row: 129.5, then 239, 237, 237, 239.
BOX_THK142 mmderived
built from BOX_RIB_H + T_BB_SKIN_TOP + T_BB_SKIN_BOT
used by LEG_BELOW_BOX, TONGUE_FULL_H, BOX_FRONT_CAP_H
The box's vertical thickness, 144. FLAG: IT WAS 138 ON TWO EQUAL 19 SKINS and it is 144 since the top went to 25 on 2026-10-11 - if 138 appears anywhere in the prose it is stale. The skins are summed separately rather than doubled BECAUSE THEY ARE NO LONGER THE SAME, and an expression reading 2 x skin is exactly how the asymmetry would get quietly lost. Named THK rather than depth because 'depth' was ambiguous against the fore-aft run.
T_REAR_PLATE6.19 mmmeasured
used by BOX_FRONT_OVERHANG, SIDE_TONGUE_START, CASTING_INBOARD_RUN, INTERPOSER_PROUD, TT_BED_Y0, REAR_PLATE_BEARING_INSET, BF12_FACE_PROTRUDE_REAR
source Specialty Metals Corp sales order W053768, 2026-10-08
6.19 - MICROMETER, the delivered plate. The sales order line reads CPL 1/4in x 11-7/8in x 47.6562in HR, which a true 1/4in puts at 6.35, so the plate is 0.16 under nominal - hot-rolled runs under. SETTLED: THE SCALE AGREES AND THE ORDER SHEET'S WEIGHT DOES NOT. That sheet put the plate at 80 lb, which would need half-inch stock over this outline. Weighed, it is 38 lb 10 oz against 39.11 computed from this thickness - 1.2 percent - and the side plate is 18 against 17.67, 1.9 percent. Two instruments on the plate itself agree; the paperwork was wrong. See ../commissioning/masses.md. NOTE: NOTHING IS LAID OUT FROM THIS - the plate is placed by its LEFT EDGE, so the thickness moves tongue and bearing figures and no hole.
BOX_SKIN_DIAG1712 mmmeasured
source his tape across the cut bottom skin
1712 - BOTH diagonals of the bottom skin, equal to the tape. SETTLED: IT IS WHAT THE EGG CRATE IS SQUARED TO - the crate's own diagonals, web outer corner to web outer corner, are checked against this number and racked to it before the glue grabs. FLAG: EQUAL DIAGONALS PROVE THE CORNERS, NOT THE SIDES - a panel with unequal sides reads two equal diagonals just as happily, so this is a squareness figure and not a size one. On a square panel it works back to a side of 1210.6, where BOX_W draws 1211. FLAG: IT SAYS NOTHING ABOUT TWIST - a crate can read this both ways and still be wound, which is why the glue-up stays on the flat reference and the rib edges get a straightedge before the top skin.
BOX_W1211 mmpinned
used by BASE_W, POST_SPAN_CORNERS, BOX_CAVITY_W, MID_TENON_BLOCK_X1, MID_TENON_BLOCK_X2, BACK_LAM_L, FRONT_CAP_L
1211 - box top skin across the machine, measured INSIDE the two side tongues. SETTLED: IT IS MACHINE_OUTER_W's 1211.238 ROUNDED DOWN TO A WHOLE MILLIMETRE - his instruction 2026-10-14, that a figure laid out with a tape has to be readable on one. SETTLED: DOWN AND NOT UP, AND THAT IS THE LOAD PATH: the side tongue laps each plate from outboard and is BOLTED THROUGH it, so rounding UP would open 0.12 per side between the plate's outer face and the tongue's inner face - a gap in the bolted lap. Rounding down leaves the plate 0.12 proud of the skin's edge instead, the tongue beds hard on the plate where the bolts are, and the 0.24 total lands in the glue line at the skin's end grain where nothing bears. NOTE: THE ENVELOPE ITSELF HAS NOT MOVED - MACHINE_OUTER_W is still 1211.238 and is still what the plates and beams derive from; this is the SKIN, cut to a whole number. Historic note follows, and the 1211.238 in it is the envelope rather than this. FLAG: THIS NOTE SAID 1210.73 UNTIL 2026-10-11 WHILE THE VALUE SAID 1211.238, and the two are DIFFERENT NUMBERS that the note conflated: 1210.73 is REAR_PLATE_L_ORDERED, the length the rear plate was BOUGHT at, and 1211.238 is MACHINE_OUTER_W, the flush envelope. They were the same figure under one name until T_SIDE_PLATE turned out to be 10 gauge rather than 1/8in, which grew the envelope by 0.508 and left the bought plate 0.77 short of it - see MACHINE_OUTER_W, whose own note records that carrying the envelope under the plate's name is what let Sheet 7 hang a beam station off a sheared edge. The prose across machine/ still says 1210.73 in two dozen places and nearly all of them are correctly about the REAR PLATE; only the box footprint was wrong, and it was fixed the same day. OPEN: THIS INVERTED on 2026-10-03 and the earlier reading is wrong: it was briefly recorded as 1204.38, the space INSIDE the side plates, on a reading that the box tucked between them. It does not. The side and back plates sit FLAT ON THE TOP SKIN and the tongues stand proud OUTBOARD of them, so the tongue's inside face is the plate's outer face. There is no channel and nothing is captured on two sides.
BOX_FORE_AFT1211 mmpinned
used by BOX_FRONT_OVERHANG, BOX_FRONT_BARE, POST_SPAN_FORE_AFT, BOX_CAVITY_FORE_AFT, BOX_FRONT_WALL_AT, BOX_SIDE_CAP_L
1211 - box top skin front to back, from the back tongue's inside face to the front edge, and the SAME as BOX_W: the box is exactly square. SETTLED: HIS CALL 2026-10-14, once the width became a chosen 1211 rather than a derived 1211.238 - at 1210 the depth was as close to square as the derived width allowed, and there is no longer anything to be close to. SETTLED: IT MAKES THE WHOLE EGG CRATE ONE PART. Both cavities are now 1175, so all eight ribs are the same 1175 x 100 piece with the same four notch offsets - which is what lets all eight be notched as ONE CLAMPED STACK on the sliding miter saw, where before the two directions differed by 1.24 and needed their own stop positions. The 1 it costs comes off nothing: it goes onto BOX_FRONT_OVERHANG, which is the remainder by design. FLAG: THE CHAIN IS NOW INVERTED AND THAT IS DELIBERATE - it used to be BEAM_LEN + T_REAR_PLATE + T_FIN + BOX_FRONT_OVERHANG with the overhang stated at 140, and the overall size fell out of it. He picks the overall size, so the SIZE is stated here and BOX_FRONT_OVERHANG is what falls out: 193.65, or 7.62in of apron. OPEN: THE OLD 140 WAS NOT A CHOICE ANYBODY DEFENDED, it was a round number; the apron depth now follows the box, which is the direction he reasons in. Historic note follows. OPEN: ALSO INVERTED on 2026-10-03, same reason as BOX_W: it was 983.65 on the reading that the box tucked inside the plates. It does not; the machine stands ON it. So this ADDS the plate thicknesses the old one subtracted, and adds the front overhang on top. THE FRONT HAS NO TONGUE - the box simply runs past the fins and brackets behind each fin tie it down. FLAG: THAT LAST CLAUSE WAS WRONG TWICE AND THIS IS WHERE THE ERROR SPREAD FROM: the count was never four per fin - FIN_BRACKETS was two per fin, four across - and since 2026-10-11 nothing goes BEHIND the fin at all, because the tee track bed leaves zero room there. One angle per fin, in front.

BOLT_DOUBLER_TO_PLATE22 mmpart
used by DOUBLER_BOLT_ENGAGE
M5 x 22 through the plate into the doubler - D1-D4. It crosses the plate and taps into the doubler's own THICKNESS, so the length is bounded by the far face rather than by strength. SETTLED: HIS CHECK AGAINST THE PARTS - an M5 x 25 just reaches that face, so 25 is the break-through point and a stock 22 is the size under it. FLAG: ONE LENGTH SERVES BOTH FACES even though the X carriage plate is 12.7 against the end plate's 12.19 - the half millimetre comes off DOUBLER_BOLT_ENGAGE, not off DOUBLER_BOLT_BREAKOUT.
DOUBLER_BOLT_ENGAGE9.81 mmderived
built from BOLT_DOUBLER_TO_PLATE - T_X_ENDPLATE
used by DOUBLER_BOLT_BREAKOUT
9.81 of thread in the doubler, 1.96 x D in 6061. These bolts run through the plate in SHEAR and a bolted joint carries shear by friction, so what the thread is for is preload.
DOUBLER_BOLT_BREAKOUT2.38 mmderived
built from T_DOUBLER - DOUBLER_BOLT_ENGAGE
2.38 of doubler left beyond the D bolt's tip. The hole is tapped THROUGH, so this is bolt length and not hole depth: the thread runs the full T_DOUBLER and the bolt simply stops short of the far face. Nothing mounts on that face - the Y beam's inside face is about 17 outboard of it - so a tip standing proud would be untidy rather than a clash. It is measured off the bolt that is actually recorded, so a longer one shows here.
DOUBLER_BOLT_X25 mmpinned
used by X_DOUBLER_BOLT_XC, SOLE_ROOT_WALL, DOUBLER_BOLT_X_PLATE
Sheet 2d's D1-D4 column, in THE DOUBLER'S OWN frame - the plate's is DOUBLER_BOLT_X_PLATE, 10 higher. One column in shear with full material around it. FLAG: IT IS NO LONGER THE DOUBLER'S MIDPOINT and must not be re-derived as one: at DOUBLER_H 45 the middle is 22.5, and 25 is deliberate. THE REASON IS R1-R4: they bore 20 deep from X 0 into the doubler's end face and clear the 5 mm D-bolt wall - the bolt spans 22.5 to 27.5 - by 2.5. Re-centre the column to 22.5 and the bolt spans 20 to 25, which a 20 deep hole arrives exactly on; the R row would have to shorten to about 17.5. His call: leave it at 25. Edge distances at 45 are 20 above and 25 below, which on a 5 mm hole is 17.5 and 22.5 of material. Moving it would also move D1-D4 on Sheets 2a and 2b, which share this column.
FIN_TONGUE_BOLT_125 mmpinned
used by FIN_ANGLE_T1_END
Sheet 1's T1, the outboard of the fin's two bolts. T2 is at 170. SETTLED: MEASURED FROM THE FIN'S OUTBOARD (SQUARE) EDGE - confirmed by him 2026-10-11, and everything about the fin angle depends on it: FIN_ANGLE_L, FIN_ANGLE_T1_END and the taper clearances are all computed from that edge. The alternative reading, machine X, is self-refuting - it would put T2 0.17 from the tapered edge at the angle leg's top. OPEN: THE NAME IS NOW WRONG AND IS LEFT ALONE DELIBERATELY: these stopped being TONGUE bolts on 2026-10-03 when the front tongue was deleted, and they are bracket bolts - renaming the symbol would break every sheet reference for no gain. Shares a value with DOUBLER_BOLT_X by coincidence and nothing else.

another name for EXT_BORE_INSET

Sheet 7's X 0 is the plate's LEFT EDGE, and that edge is set flush with the Y1 extrusion's OUTBOARD FACE - his call, 2026-10-10, and the side plate is NOT in it. The side plate therefore stands proud of the plate's end by its own thickness, and the plate's far end falls wherever the delivered length puts it.

Sheet 7's X 0 is the plate's LEFT EDGE, and that edge is set flush with the Y1 extrusion's OUTBOARD FACE - his call, 2026-10-10, and the side plate is NOT in it. Z_PLATE_L175 mmpinned
used by Z_M5_X_1, Z_M5_X_3
Z plate long dimension. See z-carriage.md for why it is 175 and why the width went 154 to 164.
Z_PLATE_W164 mmpinned
used by Z_M5_Y_1, Z_M5_Y_3, Z_NUT_Y_1, Z_NUT_Y_2
164. FLAG: EVERY HOLE ON THIS PLATE IS NOW A WHOLE MILLIMETRE - all 28 of them - and that only works at 164. It briefly went to 165 to chase the C-hole columns, on the belief that the rail pitch was 115; the pitch is 114 (see Z_RAIL_COL), which puts the block bolt columns on whole offsets and makes an EVEN width the one that works. At 165 the nut and clamp holes land on half millimetres instead. The width was 154 before that, and 164 because on 154 the outer M5 counterbore broke through the edge by 1.5; at 164 it clears by 4.55 on the measured 8.9 bit.
Z_M5_ROW_OFF_1-59.2 mmmeasured
First M5 row from the Z plate's centre. Measured on the X/Y kit 2026-09-30; the four rows are -59.2, -23.2, +20.8, +56.8, which is 36 / 44 / 36 - two blocks per spacer at a 36 pitch with 44 between them.
Z_M5_ROW_OFF_320.8 mmmeasured
Third M5 row from the plate's centre, the first on the second block. Same measurement as Z_M5_ROW_OFF_1.
Z_BLOCK_GROUP_SHIFT0 mmpinnedretired
0 - FLAG: RETIRED. It was 0.3, a deliberate slide of the block group to force the C-hole rows onto whole millimetres, and it existed only because the rows were being built from four loose measurements that were not symmetric. Deriving them from Z_BLOCK_CENTRE_SEP instead makes the pattern symmetric about the plate and the rows come out whole on their own. Kept at zero rather than deleted because the prose refers to it.
BLOCK_BODY_L77.09 mmderived
built from BLOCK_BUTTED / 2
used by Z_BLOCK_GAP
77.09, one HGH20 block along the rail - half the measured butted pair. FLAG: TRUST THIS OVER THE ROW MEASUREMENTS. Z_M5_ROW_OFF_1 and _3 imply the two block centres sit 80 apart when butted, which contradicts this by 2.91, and BLOCK_BUTTED is one direct reading of the pair where the rows are four separate readings. Same looseness the Y block rows already carry a warning about.
Z_BLOCK_CENTRE_SEP87 mmpinned
used by Z_BLOCK_GAP, Z_M5_X_1, Z_M5_X_3
87, the two Z block centres apart - CHOSEN, and the one number this whole pattern hangs on. It puts the four bolt rows on 26 / 62 / 113 / 149, all whole and symmetric about the plate, which is why the group shift that used to sit here is gone. FLAG: DO NOT SPECIFY THE GAP, SPECIFY THESE ROWS. The gap is not something you set - it is what happens when the blocks are bolted to rows that are already drilled.
Z_BLOCK_GAP9.91 mmderived
built from Z_BLOCK_CENTRE_SEP - BLOCK_BODY_L
used by Z_TRAVEL
9.91, the clear gap between the two block bodies. FLAG: THE GAP IS THE WHOLE REASON THE BLOCKS ARE SPREAD AT ALL - his, 2026-10-08: a 4 mm hex driver passes through the Z plate and the spacer into this gap to reach a rail bolt beneath, so the Z plate, spacers, blocks and rails can be built as one sub-assembly and then bolted to the X carriage plate by fastening the rails last. At 9.91 a 4 mm shaft has 2.96 mm of slop, which is the difference between a job and a fight. It costs 9.91 mm of Z travel, and the blocks were butted before precisely to spend none.
Z_TRAVEL235.91 mmderived
built from Z_RAIL_L - BLOCK_BUTTED - Z_BLOCK_GAP
used by TAPE_Z
235.91 mm of Z travel, down from the 245.82 the butted blocks gave. Every millimetre of gap is a millimetre of travel - that is the honest trade and z-carriage.md already said so in the other direction.
Z_M5_X_126 mmderived
built from (Z_PLATE_L / 2) - (Z_BLOCK_CENTRE_SEP / 2) - (BLOCK_PITCH_Y / 2)
26. Sheet 4's C-holes, first row, X from the plate's installed bottom. Row 2 is this plus BLOCK_PITCH_Y. DERIVED from the block separation now, where it used to be a measured row offset plus a 0.3 fudge to force a whole number - the fudge is gone because a symmetric pattern does not need one.
Z_M5_X_3113 mmderived
built from (Z_PLATE_L / 2) + (Z_BLOCK_CENTRE_SEP / 2) - (BLOCK_PITCH_Y / 2)
113, the third C-hole row. Row 4 is this plus BLOCK_PITCH_Y, so 149.
Z_M5_COL_INB41 mmderived

Z_M5_Y_19 mmderived

Z_M5_Y_3123 mmderived
