TENON_HOLE_CLEAR1 mmnominal
1 per side, so every tenon hole in the bottom skin is cut 2 LARGER THAN THE TENON. His, 2026-10-11, asking whether the holes are generous - and they should be. SETTLED: THE TENON IS LOCATION ONLY and a 565 lb box is being lowered onto three of them: zero clearance makes assembly impossible, and 1 per side is nothing against a job that is only stopping the box walking. The box's position on the posts is NOT a machine reference - the machine is referenced to the top skin. FLAG: WHAT MUST NOT BE GENEROUS IS THE LEDGE DEPTH, which is the bearing surface and is T_BOX_LEDGE. The hole size is free; the shoulder is not.
TENON_SPACER_GAP12 mmderived
built from POST_W_X - T_BOX_LEDGE - LEG_STRIP
12 - the air between a corner leg's tenon tab and the inner face of the perimeter web beside it. SETTLED: IT IS THE PART OF THE UN-LEDGED BAND THE SLOT TAKES. The band left standing when the T_BOX_LEDGE is cut off the side face is POST_W_X - T_BOX_LEDGE = 30 across, the tab is the one LEG_STRIP of it that is solid, and the other 12 is slot. So the spacer makes the composite tenon fill the WHOLE band - and the band's inner edge is the web's inner face, because the ledge is as wide as the wall and the leg's outer face is flush with the wall's. Written as GRID_MEMBER_T + LEG_STRIP - T_BOX_TONGUE - T_BOX_WALL_WEB it comes to the same 12 by the long way round, through the wall. SETTLED: IT IS 12 AND NOT 18 - his question off the model, 2026-10-15, answered by measuring the model: the tab's outer face is the slot's inner edge and the web's inner face is T_BOX_WALL_WEB in from the skin's edge, so the gap is what the leg's own 18 + 36 leaves after the wall's 24 + 18. THE 24 IS WHY: the leg's outer face stands flush with the TONGUE's outer face, and the tongue is 24 where every other lamination is 18, so the leg and its slot sit 6 further outboard than an all-18 wall would put them. Expect 18, get 12. FLAG: IT IS A RESIDUE, NOT A CHOICE - nothing was set to make it 12, so it MOVES if the tongue, the web, the slot or the strip does. The spacer that fills it is T_TENON_SPACER.
T_TENON_SPACER12.003 mmmeasured
12.003 - micrometer, his 12 Baltic birch scrap, 2026-10-15. It fills TENON_SPACER_GAP, glued to the OUTER face of each rear corner leg's tenon tab, POST_TENON_L tall by TENON_CORNER_Y deep. SETTLED: WHAT IT BUYS IS A CORNER AND NOT A FACE - his words. Without it the box's corner is located in one direction only: the tenon butts the back web and has 12 of air to the side web. With it the composite tenon's outer face lands ON the side web's inner face, so the tongue corner is captured both ways. NOTE: THE STOCK IS THE GAP - a nominal 12 sheet mikes at 12.003, which is why the scrap is the right thing to use rather than a piece planed to fit. FLAG: THE TWO REAR CORNER LEGS ONLY. The front centre leg's tabs already butt the front web with no gap at all.
POST_STUB_L254 mmnominal
254 - 10in, how much of each post the 3D model draws. OPEN: IT IS A DRAWING LENGTH AND NOT A PART: the posts run down to the base and nothing in this repo sets their full height. His, 2026-10-11, asked for so the tenon and ledge can be seen in context without the model growing a base.
BOX_WALL_STANDS_ON0 mmnominal
SETTLED: CLOSED 2026-10-11, AND THE MODEL WAS RIGHT: THE SKINS BUTT THE TONGUE'S INNER FACE AND THE TONGUE COVERS THEIR END GRAIN. His reasoning - the tongues are glued to the sides of the box, so there is no advantage in running the bottom skin out under them, and it is a cleaner look with the tongue capping the end. Value 0 is retained as a marker; it carries no meaning. BOX_CAVITY_W and every bay and notch position stand as drawn. FLAG: THE CLOSE EXPOSED A REAL BUG, fixed the same day: make-3d drew the tongue's bottom at -(BOX_THK - T_BB_SKIN_TOP). With both skins 19 that was -119, exactly the bottom skin's top face, matching the prose that the lamination STANDS ON the bottom skin. When the top skin went to 25 the expression still returned -119 BY COINCIDENCE while the bottom skin's top face moved to -125, so the tongue floated 6 above it. It now runs -BOX_THK to +60. A formula that reached for the bottom skin through the top skin's symbol only worked while the two were equal - which is the same failure the BOX_THK note warns about. OPEN: WHAT THE PROSE SAID: 180 mm tall, standing on the bottom skin and 60 mm proud. The 180 never closed - 100 cavity + 19 bottom skin + 60 proud is 179 - and on the settled geometry the figure is TONGUE_FULL_H = 204. The old note read: torsion-box.md says the outer lamination is '180 mm tall standing on the bottom skin and 60 mm proud' of the top skin. 180 = 19 bottom skin is NOT in it: 100 cavity + 19 bottom skin + 60 proud = 179, so 180 only closes if the lamination starts at the bottom skin's TOP face and the figure is rounded, OR if it starts at its underside and runs 60 proud of a 19 top skin. OPEN: AND THE MODEL DRAWS IT ALONGSIDE THE SKINS, NOT ON THEM - the box body spans 0 to BOX_W and the tongues sit outboard at -25 to 0, so the skins do not pass under the tongue at all. BOX_CAVITY_W and every bay and notch position depend on which reading is right. Value 0 is a placeholder so the registry resolves; it carries no meaning. ASK HIM: does the bottom skin run out under the outer lamination, or butt its inner face?
BOX_FRONT_WALL_AT1211 mmalias
another name for BOX_FORE_AFT

FIN_BOLT_TAP_DEPTH10 mmalias
another name for T_FIN

FIN_ANGLE_SKIN_FIX3 mmpart
used by FIN_ANGLE_BOLT_END_MARGIN
THREE bolts per angle, through its horizontal leg into PRONGED T-NUTS in the top skin - four was carried for one commit and he cut it to three as unnecessary over 170, driven into a drilled hole from UNDERNEATH and hammered home. His, 2026-10-11, replacing screws into plywood. NOTE: THE PART IS A T-NUT - a pronged tee nut, three or four prongs. NOT a threaded insert, which has an external wood-screw thread and drives with a hex key, and NOT a hopper-feed T-nut, which is the flanged barrel with screw holes instead of prongs. The name is recorded because he asked for it and because the three get ordered interchangeably. SETTLED: THE ORIENTATION IS RIGHT AND IT IS THE THING USUALLY GOT WRONG: a T-nut goes on the face the bolt pulls TOWARD, so the flange bears on the wood and the prongs only stop it rotating. Bolt down from above, flange under the top skin. FLAG: THEY MUST GO IN BEFORE THE TOP SKIN IS GLUED TO THE RIBS. They are fitted from below and there is no access afterwards - the same hard ordering as assembly.md's rule about driving a screw into each post tenon before the skin goes on, and the same consequence for getting it wrong: a hole in the box. SETTLED: IT ANSWERS A REAL OBJECTION: with the angle in FRONT of the fin these fixings work in WITHDRAWAL if the fin tips backward, the opposite sense to the old behind-the-fin brackets, and a prong insert in the 25 top skin is a far better anchor than a screw.

CASTING_T_AT_BLOCK35 mmpart
used by CASTING_BLOCK_BOLT_L
35 - the casting's thickness where the four bolts pass through it, either side of the bearing block. His, 2026-10-11. OPEN: IT IS NOT CASTING_RAIL_H: the rails are 30 and carry a different pair of bolts, so the casting is bolted down at TWO different thicknesses and the two bolt lengths differ by exactly that 5.
CASTING_RAIL_BOLTS2 mmpart
used by INTERPOSER_TAPPED_M5
Two M5 through the casting's RAILS and into the interposer. His, 2026-10-11.
CASTING_BLOCK_BOLTS4 mmpart
used by INTERPOSER_TAPPED_M5
Four M5 through the casting either side of the BEARING BLOCK and into the interposer. His, 2026-10-11. With CASTING_RAIL_BOLTS that is six M5 holding each casting down, and six tapped holes in each interposer.
CASTING_RAIL_BOLT_L40 mmderived
built from CASTING_RAIL_H + T_INTERPOSER
M5 x 40 through the casting rails - 30 of rail plus 10 of interposer. His, 2026-10-11, and he gave the 40 and its breakdown together. SETTLED: IT CLOSES EXACTLY ON THE STACK, which is what says the INTERPOSER IS TAPPED: the bolt finishes flush with the interposer's far face, so it cannot be reaching a T-nut in the beam beyond it - there is nothing left to reach with. 10 of M5 thread is 2 x D, which is generous. NOTE: THE SAME SHAPE AS THE FIN'S M8 x 16, which is 6 of angle leg plus 10 of tapped plate: a bolt length that equals the stack is the tell that the last layer carries the thread.
CASTING_BLOCK_BOLT_L45 mmderived
built from CASTING_T_AT_BLOCK + T_INTERPOSER
M5 x 45 through the casting either side of the bearing block - 35 of casting plus 10 of interposer. His, 2026-10-11. Same construction as CASTING_RAIL_BOLT_L and the same 10 of tapped interposer; only the casting thickness differs.
INTERPOSER_TAPPED_M56 mmderived
built from CASTING_RAIL_BOLTS + CASTING_BLOCK_BOLTS
Six M5 tapped through each interposer, for the casting above it. FLAG: THE INTERPOSER CARRIES TWO SEPARATE BOLT PATTERNS AND THEY MUST NOT BE CONFUSED: these six are TAPPED M5 holding the CASTING DOWN onto it, while INTERPOSER_BOLT_ROW_1 and _2 are M8 CLEARANCE holes for the bolts that hold the INTERPOSER ITSELF to the beam's T-slots. Different thread, different direction of assembly, different part being held. OPEN: THE TAPPING IS THE WHOLE DEPTH: T_INTERPOSER is 10 and both bolt lengths end flush with its far face, so there is no margin for a bolt that runs long - and a long one would stand proud into the beam's slot face.
TNUT_THREAD_M86 mmpart
used by BOLT_FULL_ENGAGE
6 - the thread an M8 slide-in T-nut actually gives. His, 2026-10-11, and it is SHORT: a T-nut is a thin block, not a nut, so the engagement a bolt gets is 6 whatever its length. SETTLED: THAT IS WHY THE INTERPOSER BOLT IS 20 - it crosses T_INTERPOSER's 10 and still has 10 left, which is more than enough to take all 6. FLAG: IT IS ALSO WHY A LONGER BOLT BUYS NOTHING in any T-nut joint on this machine: past 6 the extra just stands through the back of the nut into the slot.
Z_RAIL_BOLT_GRIP10 mmderived
built from Z_RAIL_BOLT_L - Z_RAIL_BOLT_ENGAGE
10 - what an M5 crosses in the Z rail before it reaches the X carriage plate's tapped thread: the rail's own material under its counterbore. OPEN: DERIVED BACKWARDS FROM HIS TWO FIGURES, the 20 bolt and the 10 of engagement, because the rail's counterbore depth is not in this file. It is a real quantity and it is HIS arithmetic, not a measured rail - caliper the counterbore if it ever matters.
Z_RAIL_BOLT_L20 mmpart
used by Z_RAIL_BOLT_GRIP
M5 x 20 for the Z rails, his 2026-10-11, seven per rail and two rails.
Z_RAIL_BOLT_ENGAGE10 mmpart
used by Z_RAIL_BOLT_GRIP
10 of M5 thread in the X carriage plate, his 2026-10-11 - 2 x D. SETTLED: IT STOPS 2.7 SHORT OF THE FAR FACE on a T_X_CARRIAGE_PLATE of 12.7, so nothing breaks through the back. OPEN: THE 12.7 IS STILL A NOMINAL in this file and this clearance is only 2.7, so a plate that micrometers thin is worth knowing about before tapping.
BF12_BODY_AT_BOLT20 mmderived
built from BF12_FACE_BOLT_L - BF12_FACE_ENGAGE_TONGUE
used by BF12_FACE_PROTRUDE_REAR
20 - how much BF12 body an M5 crosses before it reaches the plate it threads into, face-mounted. OPEN: DERIVED BACKWARDS FROM HIS TWO FIGURES - an M5 x 30 giving 10 of engagement in the X tongue - because BF12's own body dimension at the bolt is not in this file. It is HIS arithmetic, not a measured casting. SETTLED: IT IS WHAT EXPLAINS THE PROTRUSION: the same 30 bolt has the same 10 past the body wherever it lands, so in the 1/2in tongue it stops 2.19 short and in the 1/4in rear plate it runs 3.65 through.
BF12_FACE_BOLT_L30 mmpart
used by BF12_BODY_AT_BOLT, BF12_FACE_PROTRUDE_REAR
M5 x 30 for a face-mounted BF12, his 2026-10-11.
BF12_FACE_ENGAGE_TONGUE10 mmpart
used by BF12_BODY_AT_BOLT
10 of M5 thread into the X end plate's tongue, his 2026-10-11 - 2 x D in 1/2in plate, stopping 2.19 short of the far face.
BF12_FACE_PROTRUDE_REAR3.81 mmderived
built from BF12_FACE_BOLT_L - BF12_BODY_AT_BOLT - T_REAR_PLATE
3.65 - how far a face-mount M5 x 30 stands through the back of the 1/4in rear plate. His figure was 'about 4' and this is it exactly. OPEN: IT IS THE SAME BOLT AS THE TONGUE'S and that is the whole point: one length, two plate thicknesses, and only one of them stops short. NOTE: IF THE STUB IS UNWANTED, SHORTEN THE BOLT RATHER THAN CAP IT - an M5 x 25 leaves 5 of engagement in the 6.35 plate, which is 1 x D in steel, and nothing protrudes.
M8_SOCKET_CBORE8 mmpart
used by SHELF_VERT_GRIP
8 - the least a counterbore can be and still bury an M8 socket head, which is 8 tall. His, 2026-10-11. SETTLED: IT IS A GRIP TERM, NOT A DETAIL: a counterbore REMOVES material the bolt would otherwise pass through, so it makes the grip SHORTER and the bolt reach FURTHER past the far face. The shelf bar's verticals are the only counterbored joint on the machine.
SHELF_VERT_GRIP42.8 mmderived
built from SHELF_BAR_H - M8_SOCKET_CBORE
42.8 - what the shelf bar's VERTICAL bolts cross: the bar's HEIGHT less the counterbore. FLAG: IT IS SHELF_BAR_H AND NOT SHELF_BAR_W, and the fastener schedule had the width. The verticals are counterbored FROM BELOW and run up through the 50.8 height into the beam's underside slot; it is the HORIZONTAL pair that cross the 25.4 width. Reading the wrong one made an M8 x 50 look 26.6 too long when it is right.

limit sensors

LIMIT_SENSOR_H17.75 mmmeasured
used by LIMIT_SENSOR_STACK_H
source measured 2026-10-13
The SN04-N's body height - his caliper 2026-10-13. SQUARE BODY, NOT A BARREL: these are the block type, so the mount clamps the body rather than threading an M18 nut either side, and there is no barrel length to trade against a lock nut. It is the height that sets how far the sensor stands above whatever it is bolted to, which is what the stepper frame's window has to clear.
LIMIT_SENSOR_W18.35 mmmeasured
source measured 2026-10-13
The SN04-N's body width - his caliper 2026-10-13.
LIMIT_SENSOR_D35 mmmeasured
used by LIMIT_SENSOR_STACK_D
source measured 2026-10-13
The SN04-N's body depth, SENSING FACE TO WIRE EXIT - his caliper 2026-10-13. FLAG: IT INCLUDES NO CABLE BEND RADIUS. The cable leaves the back face, so the space the part really occupies fore-aft is this plus whatever the wire needs to turn, and that is the figure a pocket or a window has to clear rather than the 35.
LIMIT_SENSOR_BRACKET_D20 mmmeasured
used by LIMIT_SENSOR_STACK_D
20 - what the metal bracket adds to the sensor's own depth, his 2026-10-13. FLAG: IT IS ADDED DEPTH, NOT THE BRACKET'S OWN SIZE: the sensor overlaps it, so the pair occupy LIMIT_SENSOR_STACK_D rather than the two parts summed.
LIMIT_SENSOR_ADJUST15 mmmeasured
15 of fore-aft adjustment in the bracket's slots, his 2026-10-13. SETTLED: THIS IS WHAT MAKES THE TRIP POINT A SETTING RATHER THAN A DIMENSION - the M3 pair in the casting's top face are fixed at LIMIT_SENSOR_M3_PITCH, and the slot takes up where the target actually lands. Nothing on a drawing has to predict the trip point to better than this.
LIMIT_SENSOR_M3_PITCH8 mmpart
8 - the centres of the two M3 tapped into the stepper frame's top face, matching the bracket's slots. His scheme 2026-10-13. The frame is ferrous, so these are tapped into cast iron rather than aluminium - see machine/assembly.md.
LIMIT_SENSOR_STACK_D55 mmderived
built from LIMIT_SENSOR_D + LIMIT_SENSOR_BRACKET_D
55 - sensor plus bracket, fore-aft. OPEN: IT STILL CARRIES NO CABLE BEND. LIMIT_SENSOR_D is measured to the WIRE EXIT, not to the back of a turned cable, so a pocket or a window sized on this is sized on the metal and not on the installation.
LIMIT_SENSOR_BRACKET_H10 mmmeasured
used by LIMIT_SENSOR_STACK_H
10 - what the bracket adds in HEIGHT, his 2026-10-13, AS FLOPPED FOR THE FIRST TEST: the sensor sits ON TOP of the bracket rather than beneath it. SETTLED: BOTH ORIENTATIONS MOUNT, his 2026-10-14 - right side up as photographed fouls nothing anywhere, and inverted, nestled down inside the stepper casting frame, works too provided the sensor is held LIMIT_SENSOR_BK12_SETBACK clear of the BK12. So the bench test decides on SENSING alone and not on fit. FLAG: THE FLOP ITSELF IS STILL NOT SETTLED - it was made to get the sensing face away from the ferrous stepper frame, and whether that was necessary is the bench test. If the sensor goes back under the bracket this figure still applies but the stack height does not.
LIMIT_SENSOR_BK12_SETBACK10 mmpinned
source His, 2026-10-14
10 - the LEAST the sensor may stand back from the BACK side of the BK12 bearing, and it applies ONLY to the inverted mount: his, 2026-10-14. SETTLED: IT IS WHAT MAKES THE INVERTED OPTION REAL - turned upside down the sensor nestles down inside the stepper casting frame rather than standing LIMIT_SENSOR_STACK_H above it, and at 10 clear of the BK12 it still works. NOTE: RIGHT SIDE UP, AS PHOTOGRAPHED, HAS NO SUCH CONSTRAINT - it fouls nothing anywhere. So this is the price of the option that gives the window its height back, not a constraint on the design as drawn.
LIMIT_SENSOR_STACK_H27.75 mmderived
built from LIMIT_SENSOR_BRACKET_H + LIMIT_SENSOR_H
27.75 - how far the sensor's top stands above the surface the bracket bolts to, flopped. THIS IS THE FIGURE THE WINDOW HAS TO CLEAR if the sensor sits over the part of the casting that passes through the plate.

the rear plate's windows

WINDOW_CORNER_R10 mmpinned
used by WINDOW_CORNER_MARGIN
10 - the corner radius on each rear plate window. FLAG: THE BINDING FEATURE IS A BOLT HEAD, NOT THE CASTING. The M5 x 35 rails bolt's head sits nearest the bottom-outboard corner, and WINDOW_CORNER_MARGIN is what is left between them. OPEN: THE MARGIN SHRINKS AS THE RADIUS GROWS - 1.2 at this 10, about 2.6 at a 6 - so a smaller radius is the safer direction, not the tighter one. The geometric limit is about 13, where the head touches the fillet.
CAST_HEAD_X_IN2.76 mmderived
built from BF12_COL_INB - (M5_HEAD_D / 2)
used by WINDOW_CORNER_MARGIN
2.76 - how far in from the window's OUTBOARD edge the M5 x 35 rails bolt's head starts. The column is 7 off the beam's outer face and the window's outboard edge is that same face, so the head's own radius is all that separates them.
CAST_HEAD_Z_UP5 mmderived
built from Y_BEAM_UNDERSIDE - T_INTERPOSER - CASTING_RAIL_H - M5_HEAD_H - WINDOW_Z_LOW
used by WINDOW_CORNER_MARGIN
5 - how far the same head's underside sits above the window's BOTTOM edge. Beam underside, less the bar, less the casting's rail depth, less the head itself.
WINDOW_CORNER_MARGIN1.2013 mmderived
built from WINDOW_CORNER_R - (((WINDOW_CORNER_R - CAST_HEAD_X_IN) ** 2 + (WINDOW_CORNER_R - CAST_HEAD_Z_UP) ** 2) ** 0.5)
1.2 - metal between the M5 x 35 head and the corner fillet, as the distance from the fillet's centre to the head's nearest point taken off the radius. FLAG: IT MUST NOT GO NEGATIVE. Negative means the fillet has eaten into the head's footprint and the bolt cannot be driven. Nothing bears here, so 1.2 is a clearance rather than a stress question - but it is the figure that moves if the radius, the bolt column or the head size changes, and it is checked rather than remembered.
SOLE_TIP_W40 mmalias
another name for NUT_FACE_Y
used by SOLE_STEM_W
The sole bracket's width at its tip, and at its stem - the T is carried straight out. IT IS THE NUT'S OWN WIDTH, his call: the tip takes the nut's four M5 and nothing else is on it, so the plate is as wide as the thing it bolts to. The M5 pattern is NUT_PATTERN_Y across this, which leaves 8 to the edge. FLAG: IT WAS PINNED AT 46 AS A FREE CHOICE and is derived now, so it FOLLOWS the nut if the nut ever changes.

the nut sole brackets, as T

SOLE_BASE_D12.19 mmalias
another name for T_DOUBLER
used by SOLE_T_CHECK
12.19 - the T's base depth, outboard from the end plate's OUTER face. NOT A CHOICE: it is the doubler's end face, which is the whole of what the root bolts land on.
SOLE_STEM_W40 mmalias
another name for SOLE_TIP_W
40 - the T's stem width, carried straight out from the base rather than tapering. It is SOLE_TIP_W because the tip takes the nut's four bolts and nothing else constrains it. That leaves 8 to the edge beside an M5 at plus or minus 12, which is plenty in steel - his.
SOLE_STEM_L73.81 mmalias
another name for SOLE_REACH
used by SOLE_T_CHECK
73.81 - how far the stem runs past the base to reach the nut. SOLE_BASE_D + SOLE_STEM_L closes on SOLE_BRACKET_L, which is the check that the T covers the same reach the trapezoid did.
SOLE_FILLET_R6 mmpinned
6 - the radius where the stem meets the base, both sides. His, 2026-10-13, for looks rather than for stress, and a waterjet path walks it for nothing. FLAG: THE 3D MODEL DRAWS THIS CORNER SQUARE. Its prism profile is a polygon and a fillet would be an approximation that then disagrees with the sheet; the radius lives here and on the drawing instead.
SOLE_T_CHECK0 mmderived
built from SOLE_BRACKET_L - (SOLE_BASE_D + SOLE_STEM_L)
0 - and it must stay 0. The T's base plus its stem has to reach exactly as far as the trapezoid did, because the reach is set by the nut and not by the outline.
